\subsection{Correlation self energy}
\subsubsection{Derivation from scratch}
We can start from the definition of the GW self energy, which is a convolution of the Green's function and the screened Coulomb interaction:
\begin{align}
    \Sigma(\mathbf{k}, \omega)&=\frac{i}{2 \pi} \int d \omega^{\prime} \frac{d^3 q}{(2 \pi)^3} G\left(\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{q}, \omega-\omega^{\prime}\right) W\left(\mathbf{q}, \omega^{\prime}\right)
\end{align}
 The screened Coulomb interaction in the RPA is given by 
\begin{align}
W(\mathbf{q}, \omega)&= v(\mathbf{q}) + v(\mathbf{q}) \chi_0(\mathbf{q}, \omega) v(\mathbf{q})  + \ldots \\
&= \frac{v(\mathbf{q})}{1-v(\mathbf{q}) \chi_0{(\mathbf{q}, \omega)}} \equiv \frac{v(\mathbf{q})}{\varepsilon(\mathbf{q}, \omega)}  \\
&= v(\mathbf{q}) + v^2(\mathbf{q}) \chi_{RPA}(\mathbf{q}, \omega)\\
\implies W_{p}(\mathbf{q}, \omega) \equiv W(\mathbf{q}, \omega) - v(\mathbf{q}) &= v^2(\mathbf{q}) \chi_{RPA}(\mathbf{q}, \omega) \\
&= \sum_{\nu} \frac{2 \omega_\nu v^2(\mathbf{q}) |\rho_\nu(\mathbf{q})|^2}{\omega^2 - \omega_\nu^2}
% &= \frac{v(\mathbf{q})}{1 - v(\mathbf{q}) \sum_{\bm{k}} \frac{f_{\bm{k}+\bm{q}}-f_{\bm{k}}}{\omega+\epsilon _{\bm{k}+q}-\epsilon _{\bm{k}}+i \eta}} \\
% &= \frac{v(\mathbf{q})}{1 - \frac{1}{2}v(\mathbf{q}) \sum_k\left(f_{k+q}-f_k\right)\left[\frac{1}{\omega+\Delta_{k, q}+i \eta}-\frac{1}{\omega-\Delta_{k, q}+i \eta}\right]  }\\
% &= \frac{v(\mathbf{q})}{1 - v(\mathbf{q}) \sum_k\left(f_{k+q}-f_k\right)\frac{\Delta_{k, q}}{(\omega+i \eta)^2-\Delta_{k, q}^2}}\\
\end{align}
where it can be shown that $\varepsilon^{-1}(\mathbf{q}, \omega) = 1 + v(\mathbf{q}) \chi_{RPA}(\mathbf{q}, \omega)$. At this point, we can just quote the final result of Hedin for a retarded self energy in the $GW$ approximation:
\begin{align}
\Sigma_{p o l}\left(x, x^{\prime} ; \omega\right)&=\sum_{i, s \neq 0} \frac{V^s(\boldsymbol{r}) V^s\left(\boldsymbol{r}^{\prime}\right) \phi_i(x) \phi_i^*\left(x^{\prime}\right)}{\omega+\omega_s \operatorname{sgn}\left(\mu-\varepsilon_i\right)-\varepsilon_i} \\
\implies
    \Sigma (\bm{k}, \omega) 
    &=\sum_{q, \nu} \frac{ v^2(\mathbf{q}) |\rho_\nu(\mathbf{q})|^2}{\omega + \Omega_\nu \operatorname{sgn}(\mu - \epsilon_{\bm{k}-\bm{q}}) - \epsilon_{\bm{k}-\bm{q}} + i \eta}
\end{align}
since we know that we will have a diagonal self energy in momentum space for the UEG. We defined
\begin{align}
    V^s(\mathbf{r}) \rightarrow V^\nu(\mathbf{q}) &= v(\mathbf{q}) \rho_\nu(\mathbf{q}) , \\
    \omega_s \rightarrow \Omega_\nu 
\end{align}
In relation to our Casida eigenproblem, we can identify that the excitation energies $\Omega_\nu$ are just the eigenvalues from Eqn. \ref{eqn:casida_eq}, and the transition densities $\rho_\nu(\bm{q})$ are given by
\begin{equation}
    \rho_{\nu}(\bm{q}) = \sum_{\bm{k}_i \bm{k}_a} (X_{\bm{k}_i \bm{k}_a}^\nu(\bm{q}) + Y_{\bm{k}_i \bm{k}_a}^\nu(\bm{q})).
\end{equation}
So the expression I find has one more factor of the ERI then the one I have written below.
\begin{equation}
    \Sigma^{\text{corr}}(\bm{k}_p,\omega) = \sum_{\bm{k}_q}\sum_{\nu }^{\text{RPA}} \frac{4\pi}{|\bm{k}_q|^2} |\rho_\nu(\bm{k}_q)|^2 \left[ \frac{f_{\bm{k}_p - \bm{k}_q}}{\omega + \Omega_\nu(\bm{k}_q) - \epsilon_{\bm{k}_p - \bm{k}_q} + i\eta} + \frac{1 - f_{\bm{k}_p - \bm{k}_q}}{\omega - \Omega_\nu(\bm{k}_q) - \epsilon_{\bm{k}_p - \bm{k}_q} + i\eta} \right]
\end{equation}

\subsection{Spectral function}
Finally, the spectral function is given by
\begin{equation}
    A(\bm{k}_p,\omega) = \frac{1}{\pi} \frac{|\text{Im}\,\Sigma(\bm{k}_p,\omega)|}{\left(\omega - \epsilon_{\bm{k}_p} - \text{Re}\,\Sigma(\bm{k}_p,\omega)\right)^2 + \left(\text{Im}\,\Sigma(\bm{k}_p,\omega)\right)^2}
\end{equation}\subsection{Self-consistent GW}
\subsubsection{Self-energy}
At a given iteration $n$, including the one-shot level, the correlation self-energy is always constructed as
\begin{equation}
    \Sigma_{k, c}^{n,R}[\etaS](t)=\theta(t)\left[-\sum_{l, I}\left|M_{ k,l}^{I,(n)}\right|^2 G_{l}^{<,(n)}(t) e^{+i \Omega_I^{(n)} t} e^{-\etaS t} + \sum_{l, I}\left|M_{ k,l}^{I,(n)}\right|^2 G_{l}^{>,(n)}(t) e^{-i \Omega_I^{(n)} t} e^{- \etaS t}\right].
\label{eq:sigt}
\end{equation}
\subsubsection{Spectral function}
And we get the spectral function as
\begin{align}
     A_k^{(n)}(\omega) &= \frac{1}{\pi} \frac{|\text{Im}\,\Sigma_{k}^{n,R}[\etaS](\omega)|+\eta_G}{\left(\omega - \epsilon_k^0 - \text{Re}\,\Sigma_{k}^{n,R}[\etaS](\omega)\right)^2 + \left(|\text{Im}\,\Sigma_{k}^{n,R}[\etaS](\omega)|+\eta_G\right)^2} \\
&= \frac{1}{\pi} \frac{|\text{Im}\,\Sigma_{k, c}^{n,R}[\etaS](\omega)|+\eta_G}{\left(\omega - \epsilon_k^0 - \left(\text{Re}\,\Sigma_{k, c}^{n,R}[\etaS](\omega) + \Sigma_k^{x,(n)}\right)\right)^2 + \left(|\text{Im}\,\Sigma_{k, c}^{n,R}[\etaS](\omega)|+\eta_G\right)^2}
\label{eq:A}
\end{align}
 For $n\geq0$, we have $\Sigma_k^{x,(n)} = n_{k}^{(n)}v_m-\sum_{\bm{q}\neq\bm{0}} n_{\bm{k}+\bm{q}}^{(n)}v(\bm{q})$, where $v_m=-\frac{\alpha_0}{L}$. The interpretation is that the first term is Madelung potential at iteration $n$, and the second term is the exchange potential due to Ewald summation at iteration $n$. The spectral function is then used to compute the chemical potential $\mu^{(n)}$ and the lesser and greater Green's functions $G_k^{<,(n)}(t)$ and $G_k^{>,(n)}(t)$, which are then used to compute the correlation self-energy at iteration $n+1$.
\subsubsection{Green's function}
This gives us a recipe to update the chemical potential $\mu^{(n)}$ for $n>0$, where $\mu^{(0)}=\frac{\left(k^2/2 + \Gamma^1_k\right)_{\rm HOMO}+\left(k^2/2 + \Gamma^1_k\right)_{\rm LUMO}}{2}$ as
\begin{equation}
    N/2 = \sum_k \int_{-\infty}^{\mu^{(n)}} A_k^{(n)}(\omega) d \omega,
\label{eq:mu}
\end{equation}
where we can take $N/2$ as the number of occupied bands in our spin-restricted UEG. Once we have the chemical potential, we determine the lesser and greater Green's functions as
\begin{align}
    G_k^{<,(n)}(t)&=i \int_{-\infty}^{\mu^{(n)}} d \omega A_k^{(n)}(\omega) e^{-i \omega t} \\
    G_k^{>,(n)}(t)&=-i \int_{\mu^{(n)}}^{\infty} d \omega A_k^{(n)}(\omega) e^{-i \omega t} .
\end{align}
Recall that we determine the Green's functions at the zeroth iteration by
\begin{align}
    &G^R_{0,n}(t)=-i\Theta(t)e^{-i\varepsilon_n^{\rm LDA}t}\\
&\implies G^<_{0,i}(t)=ie^{-i\varepsilon_i^{\rm LDA}t}, \quad G^>_{0,a}(t)=-ie^{-i\varepsilon_a^{\rm LDA}t} \quad \forall t>0\\
&\implies \Sigma_{m, c}^{R}(t)=-i\theta(t)\left[\sum_{i, I}\left|M_{ m,i}^I\right|^2 e^{-i(\varepsilon_i^{\rm LDA}-\Omega_I) t} + \sum_{a, I}\left|M_{ m,a}^I\right|^2 e^{-i(\varepsilon_a^{\rm LDA}+\Omega_I) t}\right]\\
\implies    \Sigma_{m, c}^{R}(\omega) &= \sum_{i, I}\frac{\left|M_{ m,i}^I\right|^2}{\omega - \varepsilon_i^{\rm LDA}+\Omega_I + i\eta} + \sum_{a, I}\frac{\left|M_{ m,a}^I\right|^2}{\omega - \varepsilon_a^{\rm LDA}-\Omega_I + i\eta}.
\end{align}
\subsection{Screened interaction}
\label{sec:W}
For scGW0, we take $M^{(n)}=M^{(0)}$ and $\Omega^{(n)}=\Omega^{(0)}$ for all $n$. This has the interpretation of us freezing the interaction at the RPA level rather than updating it. For scGW, define
\begin{align}
    B^{(n)}_{\bm q}(\Omega) &\;\equiv\; -\frac{1}{\pi}\,
\mathrm{Im}\,W^{R,(n)}_{c}(\bm q,\Omega) \\
&= \frac{v_{\bm q}^{2}\, S^{0,(n)}_{\bm q}(\Omega)}
{\big[1 - v_{\bm q}\,\mathrm{Re}\,P^{(n)}_{\bm q}(\Omega)\big]^{2}
+ \big[\pi v_{\bm q} S^{0,(n)}_{\bm q}(\Omega)\big]^{2}}
\end{align}
with
\begin{align}
S^{0,(n)}_{\bm q}(\Omega) &\equiv -\frac{1}{\pi}\mathrm{Im}\,P^{R,(n)}(\bm q,\Omega)
= \frac{2}{V}\sum_{\bm k}\int\! d\epsilon\;
A^{<,(n)}_{\bm k}(\epsilon)\,A^{>,(n)}_{\bm k + \bm q}(\epsilon + \Omega),
\label{eq:S0}\\
\mathrm{Re}\,P^{(n)}_{\bm q}(\Omega) &=
\mathcal{P}\!\!\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\! d\Omega'\,
\frac{S^{0,(n)}_{\bm q}(\Omega')}{\Omega - \Omega'},
\qquad S^{0}_{\bm q}(-\Omega) = -S^{0}_{\bm q}(\Omega).
\label{eq:ReP}
\end{align}
Then,
\begin{equation}
\Sigma^{n,R}_{k,c}[\etaS](t) \;=\; \frac{\theta(t)}{V}
\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\! d\Omega \sum_{\bm l \ne \bm k}
B^{(n)}_{\bm k - \bm l}(\Omega)\,
\Big[\, -\,G^{<,(n)}_{l}(t)\, e^{+i\Omega t}
\;+\; G^{>,(n)}_{l}(t)\, e^{-i\Omega t} \,\Big] e^{-\etaS t},
\label{eq:scgw_sigma_t}
\end{equation}
with
\begin{equation}
G^{<,(n)}_{l}(t) = i\!\int_{-\infty}^{\mu^{(n)}}\!\! d\omega\,
A^{(n)}_{l}(\omega)e^{-i\omega t},
\qquad
G^{>,(n)}_{l}(t) = -i\!\int_{\mu^{(n)}}^{\infty}\!\! d\omega\,
A^{(n)}_{l}(\omega)e^{-i\omega t}.
\end{equation}
\subsubsection{Specification to scGW0}
Plugging in the identity
\begin{equation}
    \frac{1}{V} B_{\mathrm{k}-1}^{(0)}(\Omega)=\sum_I\left|M_{k, l}^{I,(0)}\right|^2 \delta\left(\Omega-\Omega_I^{(0)}\right),
\end{equation}
we get
\begin{equation}
    \Sigma_{k, c}^{n,R}[\etaS](t)=\theta(t)\left[-\sum_{l, I}\left|M_{ k,l}^{I,(n)}\right|^2 G_{l}^{<,(n)}(t) e^{+i \Omega_I^{(n)} t} e^{-\etaS t} + \sum_{l, I}\left|M_{ k,l}^{I,(n)}\right|^2 G_{l}^{>,(n)}(t) e^{-i \Omega_I^{(n)} t} e^{- \etaS t}\right].
\label{eq:sigt0}
\end{equation}


\subsection{scGW}
\subsection{Self-consistent GW}
\subsubsection{Formulation in the TDL}
The full self-energy is 
\begin{equation}
    \Sigma^{(n)}(\mathbf{k}, \omega)=\Sigma_{H F}^{(n)}(\mathbf{k})+\Sigma^{(n),c}(\mathbf{k}, \omega) 
\end{equation}
with
\begin{equation}
    \mathbf{\Sigma}_{H F}^{(n)}(\mathbf{k})=-\mathbf{\sum}_{\mathbf{q}} v(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}) n_{\mathbf{q}}^{(n)}
\end{equation}
where
\begin{equation}
n_{\mathrm{k}}^{(n)}=\int_{-\infty}^{\mu^{(n)}} A^{(n)}(\mathbf{k}, \omega) d \omega.
\end{equation}
But during the self-consistent cycle, we should update the chemical potential $\mu^{(n)}$, such that it always takes the form 
\begin{equation}
    N/2 = \sum_k \int_{-\infty}^{\mu^{(n)}} A_k^{(n)}(\omega) d \omega.
\end{equation}
Note that in the initial iteration this reduces to $\mu^{(0)}=\frac{\left(k^2/2 + \Sigma_{HF}^{(0)}\right)_{\rm HOMO}+\left(k^2/2 + \Sigma_{HF}^{(0)}\right)_{\rm LUMO}}{2}$.
% $$
% \begin{equation*}
% \Sigma(\mathbf{k}, \omega)=\Sigma_{H F}(\mathbf{k})+\Sigma^c(\mathbf{k}, \omega) . \tag{12}
% \end{equation*}
% $$
% $\Sigma_{H F}(\mathbf{k})$ (rcal) is the energy-independent Hartree-Fock (exchange) part of the self-energy given by the usual expression
% $$
% \begin{equation*}
% \mathbf{\Sigma}_{H F}(\mathbf{k})=-\mathbf{\sum}_{\mathbf{q}} v(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}) n_{\mathbf{q}}, \tag{13}
% \end{equation*}
% $$
The correlation self-energy gets computed as 
\begin{equation}
\Sigma^{(n),c}(\mathbf{k}, \omega)=\int_C \frac{\Gamma^{(n)}\left(\mathbf{k}, \omega^{\prime}\right)}{\omega-\omega^{\prime}} d \omega^{\prime}
\end{equation}
where
\begin{equation}
\Gamma^{(n)}(\mathbf{k}, \omega)=\sum_{\mathbf{q}} \int_0^\omega d \omega^{\prime} A^{(n)}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}, \omega-\omega^{\prime}\right) B^{(n)}\left(\mathbf{q}, \omega^{\prime}\right).
\end{equation}
If we were to do a calculation where we froze the interaction at the RPA level, we would actually have $B^{(n)}\equiv B_0$, where
\begin{equation}
B_0(q, \omega)=-\frac{1}{\pi} \frac{v^2(q) \operatorname{Im} \chi_0(q, \omega)}{\left|1-v(q) \chi_0(q, \omega)\right|^2} \operatorname{sgn}(\omega)
\end{equation}
with $\chi_0$ being the Lindhard function, whose value should we take from his original paper. If we want to self-consistently update the interaction, we would need to do 
% $B(\mathbf{q}, \omega)=\frac{v^2(\mathbf{q}) S_0(\mathbf{q}, \omega)}{\left|1-v(\mathbf{q}) P_0(\mathbf{q}, \omega)\right|^2}$
\begin{equation}
    B^{(n)}(\mathbf{q}, \omega)=\frac{v^2(\mathbf{q}) S_0^{(n)}(\mathbf{q}, \omega)}{\left|1-v(\mathbf{q}) P_0^{(n)}(\mathbf{q}, \omega)\right|^2}
\end{equation}
with
\begin{equation}
    P_0^{(n)}(\mathbf{q}, \omega)=\int_C \frac{S_0^{(n)}\left(\mathbf{q}, \omega^{\prime}\right)}{\omega-\omega^{\prime}} d \omega^{\prime}
\end{equation}
and 
\begin{equation}
    S_0^{(n)}(\mathbf{q}, \omega)=2 \sum_{\mathbf{k}} \int_0^\omega A^{(n)}\left(\mathbf{k}, \omega^{\prime}-\omega\right) A^{(n)}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}, \omega^{\prime}\right) d \omega^{\prime}.
\end{equation}
\textbf{We should verify numerically that in our first iteration $B^{(0)}\equiv B_0$. This implies that $S_0^{(0)}(\mathbf{q}, \omega) = -\frac{1}{\pi} \operatorname{Im} \chi_0(\mathbf{q}, \omega) \operatorname{sgn}(\omega)$, which we should verify numerically. In the paper, they also suggest checking the identities $\int_{-\infty}^{\infty} \Gamma(\mathbf{k}, \omega) d \omega=\sum_{\mathbf{q}} \int_0^{\infty} B_0(\mathbf{q}, \omega) d \omega$ and $\int_0^{\infty} B(\mathbf{q}, \omega) \omega d \omega=v^2(\mathbf{q}) \int_0^{\infty} S_0(\mathbf{q}, \omega) \omega d \omega.$ Should we check these as well; I feel that the former one should hold only in the initial iteration, but the latter one may hold throughout the self-consistency cycle?} Then we compute the spectral function via
\begin{equation}
    A^{(n)}(\mathbf{k}, \omega)=\frac{1}{\pi} \frac{\left|\operatorname{Im} \Sigma^{(n)}(\mathbf{k}, \omega)\right|}{\left|\omega-\epsilon_{\mathbf{k}}-\operatorname{Re} \Sigma^{(n)}(\mathbf{k}, \omega)\right|^2+\left|\operatorname{Im} \Sigma^{(n)}(\mathbf{k}, \omega)\right|^2}.
\end{equation}
\emph{What value should we use for $\epsilon_{\mathbf{k}}$ during the self consistency cycle? Do we just take it to be the free electron value all the time? }
\subsubsection{Formulation in the finite basis}
At iteration $n$ :
\begin{itemize}
\item[1.] Start with $G^{(n)}(\mathbf{k}, \omega)$ and compute
\begin{equation}
    A^{(n)}(\mathbf{k}, \omega)=-\frac{1}{\pi} \Im G^{(n), R}(\mathbf{k}, \omega) .
\end{equation}
\item[2.] Determine $\mu^{(n)}$ from
\begin{equation}
N / 2=\sum_{\mathbf{k}} \int^{\mu^{(n)}}_{-\infty} A^{(n)}(\mathbf{k}, \omega) d \omega .
\end{equation}
\item[3.] Update
\begin{equation}
    \Sigma_x^{(n)}(\mathbf{k})=-\sum_{\mathbf{q}} v(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}) n_{\mathbf{q}}^{(n)} .
\end{equation}
\item[4.] Construct the bubble
\begin{align}
P^{(n)}&=-i G^{(n)} G^{(n)} \\
\implies
P^{(n), R}(\mathbf{q}, \omega)&=2 \sum_{\mathbf{k}} \int_{-\infty}^{\infty} d \epsilon \int_{-\infty}^{\infty} d \epsilon^{\prime} A_{\mathbf{k}}^{(n)}(\epsilon) A_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}^{(n)}\left(\epsilon^{\prime}\right) \frac{f^{(n)}(\epsilon)-f^{(n)}\left(\epsilon^{\prime}\right)}{\omega+\epsilon-\epsilon^{\prime}+i \eta} 
\end{align}
where at zero temperature $f^{(n)}(\epsilon)=\Theta\left(\mu^{(n)}-\epsilon\right)$.
\item[5.] Solve
\begin{align}
W_c^{(n),R}(\mathbf{q}, \omega)&=\left[1-v(\mathbf{q}) P^{(n), R}(\mathbf{q}, \omega)\right]^{-1} v(\mathbf{q}) - v(\mathbf{q}) \\
\implies B^{(n)}(\mathrm{q}, \omega)&=-\frac{1}{\pi} \operatorname{Im} W_c^{(n), R}(\mathrm{q}, \omega)
\end{align}
\item[6.] Compute
\begin{align}
    \Sigma_{c}^{(n+1),R}(\mathbf{k}, \omega)
    &=
    \sum_{\mathbf{q}}
    \int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\epsilon\,
    A_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{q}}^{(n)}(\epsilon)
    \int_0^\infty d\Omega\,
    B_{}^{(n)}(\mathbf{q}, \Omega)
    \nonumber\\
    &\qquad\times
    \left[
    \frac{
        f^{(n)}(\epsilon)
    }{
        \omega-\epsilon+\Omega+i\eta
    }
    +
    \frac{
        1-f^{(n)}(\epsilon)
    }{
        \omega-\epsilon-\Omega+i\eta
    }
    \right].
\end{align}
\item[8.] Solve the Dyson equation
\begin{align}
    G^{(n+1),R}(\mathbf{k}, \omega)&=\left[G_0^{-1, R}(\mathbf{k}, \omega)-\Sigma_x^{(n)}(\mathbf{k})-\Sigma_c^{(n+1,R)}(\mathbf{k}, \omega)\right]^{-1}.
\end{align}
where
\begin{equation}
\left[G_0^R(\mathbf{k}, \omega)\right]^{-1}=\omega-\frac{k^2}{2}+i \eta .
\end{equation}
or
\begin{equation}
\left[G_0^R(\mathbf{k}, \omega)\right]^{-1}=\omega+\mu^{(n)}-\frac{k^2}{2}+i \eta .
\end{equation}
\emph{I believe that we should use the first definition for the non-interacting Green's function if we are using an absolute energy axis. }
% $$
% G^{(n+1)}=\left[G_0^{-1}-\Sigma_x^{(n)}-\Sigma_c^{(n+1)}\right]^{-1} .
% $$
\item[9.] Repeat until convergence. Then plot
\begin{equation}
    A(\mathbf{k}, \omega)=-\frac{1}{\pi} \Im G^{R}(\mathbf{k}, \omega) .
\end{equation}
\end{itemize}
\subsection{GW+2SOSEX-psd}
I have also implemented GW+2SOSEX-psd. This is a vertex-corrected version (approximating G3W2) recently proposed by Bruneval et al. that is guaranteed to give a positive spectral function. The self-energy is given in HF MO basis, with an infinitesimal broadening $\eta_{\Sigma}$, by
% $$
% \Sigma_{p q}^{G W+2 \mathrm{SOSEX}-\mathrm{psd}, o}=\sum_{k s} \frac{\left(w_s^{p k}+\tilde{w}_s^{p k}\right) \cdot\left(w_s^{q k}+\tilde{w}_s^{q k}\right)}{\omega-\epsilon_k+\Omega_s-\mathrm{i} \eta}
% $$
% $$
% \Sigma_{p q}^{G W+2 \mathrm{SOSEX}-\mathrm{psd}, v}=\sum_{c s} \frac{\left(w_s^{p c}+\tilde{w}_s^{p c}\right) \cdot\left(w_s^{q c}+\tilde{w}_s^{q c}\right)}{\omega-\epsilon_c-\Omega_s+\mathrm{i} \eta}
% $$
\begin{equation}
    \Sigma_{pp}^{G W+2 \mathrm{SOSEX}-\mathrm{psd}}(\omega)=\sum_{k s} \frac{\left(w_{s, \rm HF}^{p k}+\tilde{w}_{s, \rm HF}^{p k}\right)^2}{\omega-\bar{\epsilon_k^{\rm HF}}+\Omega_s^{\rm HF}-\mathrm{i} \eta_{\Sigma}}+\sum_{c s} \frac{\left(w_{s, \rm HF}^{p c}+\tilde{w}_{s, \rm HF}^{p c}\right)^2}{\omega-\bar{\epsilon_c^{\rm HF}}-\Omega_s^{\rm HF}+\mathrm{i} \eta_{\Sigma}}
\end{equation}
with
\begin{equation}
    \tilde{w}_{s, \rm HF}^{p c}=\sum_{a j} w_{s, \rm HF}^{a j} \cdot\left[\frac{(p a|j c)}{\bar{\epsilon_a^{\rm HF}}-\bar{\epsilon_j^{\rm HF}}-\Omega_s^{\rm HF}}+\frac{(p j | a c)}{\bar{\epsilon_a^{\rm HF}}-\bar{\epsilon_j^{\rm HF}}+\Omega_s^{\rm HF}}\right].
\end{equation}
Note that the equations above assume dRPA@HF screening. \emph{Should the $\bar{\epsilon}$ be the Madelung-corrected HF orbital energies or no? My suspicion is maybe not, as this would not be consistent with how we comppute the GW self-energy in the DFT case.} Then, with the finite broadening $\eta_G$, I obtain the spectral function as 
\begin{equation}
    A_p(\omega)=\frac{1}{\pi} \frac{\left|\operatorname{Im} \Sigma_{pp}^{G W+2 \mathrm{SOSEX}-\mathrm{psd}}(\omega)+\eta_G\right|}{\left|\omega-\epsilon_p^0-\operatorname{Re} \Sigma_{pp}^{G W+2 \mathrm{SOSEX}-\mathrm{psd}}(\omega)-\Sigma_{pp}^x\right|^2+\left|\operatorname{Im} \Sigma_{pp}^{G W+2 \mathrm{SOSEX}-\mathrm{psd}}(\omega)+\eta_G\right|^2}
\end{equation}
We should try this also with the LDA starting point. Note that if we are starting from LDA, we should be using dRPA@LDA screening and then
\begin{equation}
    \Sigma_{pp}^{G W+2 \mathrm{SOSEX}-\mathrm{psd}}(\omega)=\sum_{k s} \frac{\left(w_{s, \rm LDA}^{p k}+\tilde{w}_{s, \rm LDA}^{p k}\right)^2}{\omega-\epsilon_k^{\rm LDA}+\Omega_s^{\rm LDA}-\mathrm{i} \eta_{\Sigma}}+\sum_{c s} \frac{\left(w_{s, \rm LDA}^{p c}+\tilde{w}_{s, \rm LDA}^{p c}\right)^2}{\omega-\epsilon_c^{\rm LDA}-\Omega_s^{\rm LDA}+\mathrm{i} \eta_{\Sigma}}
\end{equation}
with
\begin{equation}
    \tilde{w}_{s, \rm LDA}^{p c}=\sum_{a j} w_{s, \rm LDA}^{a j} \cdot\left[\frac{(p a|j c)}{\epsilon_a^{\rm LDA}-\epsilon_j^{\rm LDA}-\Omega_s^{\rm LDA}}+\frac{(p j | a c)}{\epsilon_a^{\rm LDA}-\epsilon_j^{\rm LDA}+\Omega_s^{\rm LDA}}\right].
\end{equation}
Thus, with the finite broadening $\eta_G$, I obtain the spectral function as 
\begin{equation}
    A_p(\omega)=\frac{1}{\pi} \frac{\left|\operatorname{Im} \Sigma_{pp}^{G W+2 \mathrm{SOSEX}-\mathrm{psd}}(\omega)+\eta_G\right|}{\left|\omega-\epsilon_p^0-\operatorname{Re} \Sigma_{pp}^{G W+2 \mathrm{SOSEX}-\mathrm{psd}}(\omega)-\Sigma_{pp}^x\right|^2+\left|\operatorname{Im} \Sigma_{pp}^{G W+2 \mathrm{SOSEX}-\mathrm{psd}}(\omega)+\eta_G\right|^2}
\end{equation}
The authors of this method say that "in the course of  this study,  we also  evaluated the complete spectral  functions,  but we found that the satellite structures in the spectral functions were very sensitive to the technical details, basis set, mean-field starting point.  In our opinion, they are not conclusive pieces of information." 

% $$
% \begin{equation*}
% \epsilon_{\mathrm{k}}=\frac{1}{2} k^2-\mu, \tag{20}
% \end{equation*}
% $$
% with
% $$
% \begin{equation*}
% \mu=\frac{1}{2} k_F^2+\Sigma\left(k_F, 0\right) . \tag{21}
% \end{equation*}
% $$

% Using the spectral representations given by the Eqs. (6) and (11), Eq. (19) can more conveniently be written in terms of the spectral functions $A(\mathbf{k}, \omega)$ and $\Gamma(\mathbf{k}, \omega)$,
% $$
% \begin{equation*}
% A(\mathbf{k}, \omega)=\frac{\Gamma(\mathbf{k}, \omega)}{\left|\omega-\epsilon_{\mathbf{k}}-\Sigma(\mathbf{k}, \omega)\right|^2} . \tag{22}
% \end{equation*}
% $$
\section{GW+C}
\subsection{Cumulant}
 By relating the Dyson equation to the Taylor series expansion of the exponential (both to first order), we can write:
\begin{equation}
    \bm{G}^0(t) \bm{C}(t) = \iint \dd t_1 \dd t_2 \bm{G}^0(t-t_1) \bm{\Sigma}^c(t_1 - t_2) \bm{G}^0(t_2)
\end{equation}
We know that all of the operators are diagonal in momentum space for the UEG, so insertion of the resolution of the identity just gives us:
\begin{align}
\bm{G}^{0}(\bm{p}, t) \bm{C}(\bm{p}, t) &= {\iint \dd t_1 \dd t_2 \bm{G}^{0}(\bm{p}, t-t_1) \bm{\Sigma}^c(\bm{p}, t_1 - t_2) \bm{G}^{0}(\bm{p}, t_2)} \\
&= \int \frac{\dd \omega}{2\pi} e^{-i\omega t} \bm{G}^{0}(\bm{p},\omega)\bm{\Sigma}^c(\bm{p},\omega)\bm{G}^{0}(\bm{p},\omega) \\
\implies \bm{C}(\bm{p}, t) &= i \int \frac{\dd \omega}{2\pi} \frac{ \bm{\Sigma}^c(\bm{p},\omega+\epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{HF})}{(\omega + i \eta)^2} e^{-i \omega t} \\
&= i \int \frac{d\omega}{2\pi} \frac{1}{(\omega + i \eta)^2} e^{-i \omega t} \Biggl\{\sum_{\bm{q}}\sum_{\nu }^{\text{RPA}} \underbrace{\frac{4\pi}{|\bm{q}|^2} |\rho_\nu(\bm{q})|^2 }_{M_{\bm{q}\nu}^2}\left[ \frac{f_{\bm{p} - \bm{q}}}{\omega \underbrace{+ \Omega_\nu(\bm{q}) + (\epsilon_{\bm{p}} - \epsilon_{\bm{p} - \bm{q}}) + i\eta}_{-\Delta ^{\text{occ}}_{\bm{q}\nu}}}\right. \\
& \left. + \frac{1 - f_{\bm{p} - \bm{q}}}{\omega \underbrace{- \Omega_\nu(\bm{q})+ (\epsilon_{\bm{p}} - \epsilon_{\bm{p} - \bm{q}} )+ i\eta}_{-\Delta^{\text{virt}}_{\bm{q}\nu}}} \right]\Biggr\} \notag \\
&= i \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} M_{\bm{q}\nu}^2 \int \frac{d\omega}{2\pi} e^{-i \omega t} \left[\frac{f_{\bm{p} - \bm{q}}}{(\omega + i \eta)^2(\omega - \Delta^{\text{occ}}_{\bm{q}\nu})} + \frac{1 - f_{\bm{p} - \bm{q}}}{(\omega + i \eta)^2(\omega - \Delta^{\text{virt}}_{\bm{q}\nu})}\right] \\
&= \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ}} \left[e^{-i \Delta^{\text{occ}}_{\bm{q}\nu} t} - 1 + i \Delta^{\text{occ}}_{\bm{q}\nu} t\right] + \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{virt}} \left[e^{-i \Delta^{\text{virt}}_{\bm{q}\nu} t} - 1 + i \Delta^{\text{virt}}_{\bm{q}\nu} t\right]
\end{align}
For the final expression, we have defined $\zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ}} = \left(\frac{f_{\bm{p}-\bm{q}} M_{\bm{q}\nu}}{\Delta^{\text{occ}}_{\bm{q}\nu}}\right)^2$ and $\zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{virt}} = \left(\frac{(1-f_{\bm{p}-\bm{q}}) M_{\bm{q}\nu}}{\Delta^{\text{virt}}_{\bm{q}\nu}}\right)^2$. This allows us to arrive at the something similar to the Landau form of the cumulant.
%  For the final expression, we have defined $\zeta_{pi\nu} = \left(\frac{M_{pi\nu}}{\Delta_{pi\nu}}\right)^2$ and $\zeta_{pa\nu} = \left(\frac{M_{pa\nu}}{\Delta_{pa\nu}}\right)^2$. This allows us to arrive at the something similar to the Landau form of the cumulant.
\begin{tcolorbox}
A few notes on how to evaluate the contour integral: there is a double pole at $\omega_1 = -i\eta$ and a simple pole at $\omega_2 = -\Delta - i\eta$. Closing the contour in the lower half-plane because $\operatorname{Im}\left(\omega_1\right), \operatorname{Im}\left(\omega_2\right)<0$, and applying Cauchy's residue theorem, leads to
\begin{align}
    \int \frac{\mathrm{d} \omega}{2 \pi} e^{-\mathrm{i} \omega t} \frac{1}{\left(\omega-\omega_1\right)^2} \frac{1}{\omega-\omega_2} & =(-\mathrm{i})\left\{\left[\partial_\omega\left(\frac{e^{-\mathrm{i} \omega t}}{\omega-\omega_2}\right)\right]_{\omega=\omega_1}+\left[\frac{e^{-\mathrm{i} \omega t}}{\left(\omega-\omega_1\right)^2}\right]_{\omega=\omega_2}\right\} \\
    & =\frac{(-\mathrm{i})}{\left(\omega_1-\omega_2\right)^2}\left\{\left[(-\mathrm{i} t)\left(\omega_1-\omega_2\right)-1\right] e^{-\mathrm{i} \omega_1 t}+e^{-\mathrm{i} \omega_2 t}\right\}\\
\implies \int \frac{\mathrm{d} \omega}{2 \pi} e^{-i \omega t} \frac{1}{[\omega-(0-\mathrm{i} \eta)]^2} \frac{f}{\omega-\Delta}&=\frac{-\mathrm{i}f}{\Delta^2}\left(e^{-\mathrm{i} \Delta t}+\mathrm{i} \Delta t-1\right)
\end{align}
\end{tcolorbox}
\subsection{Green's function}
Now, we plug in our derived expression for $C(\bm{p}, t)$ into the cumulant ansatz for the retarded Green's function:
\begin{align}
G^{GW+C}(\bm{p}, t) & = G^{HF}(\bm{p}, t) e^{C(\bm{p}, t)} \\
& = -i \Theta(t) e^{-i \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{HF} t + C(\bm{p}, t)} \\
& = -i \Theta(t) e^{-i \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{HF} t + \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ}}\left(e^{-i\Delta^{\text{occ}}_{\bm{q}\nu} t} + i\Delta^{\text{occ}}_{\bm{q}\nu} t - 1\right) + \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{virt}} \left(e^{-i\Delta^{\text{virt}}_{\bm{q}\nu} t} + i\Delta^{\text{virt}}_{\bm{q}\nu} t - 1\right)}\\
&= -i \Theta (t) Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP} e^{-i \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP} t} e^{\sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ}} e^{-i\Delta^{\text{occ}}_{\bm{q}\nu} t} + \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{virt}} e^{-i\Delta^{\text{virt}}_{\bm{q}\nu} t}} \\
\end{align}
where we have the weight of the quasiparticle peak $Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP} = \exp\left(-\sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ}} - \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{virt}}\right)$ and the quasiparticle energy $\epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP} = \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{HF} - \left(\sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ}}\Delta^{\text{occ}}_{\bm{q}\nu} + \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{virt}}\Delta^{\text{virt}}_{\bm{q}\nu}\right)$.
\begin{tcolorbox}
We pause to make some important connections. Notice
\begin{align}
    Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP} & = \exp\left(-\sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ}} - \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{virt}}\right) \\
    & = \exp\left(-\sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \left(\frac{f_{\bm{p}-\bm{q}} M_{\bm{q}\nu}}{\Delta^{\text{occ}}_{\bm{q}\nu}}\right)^2 - \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \left(\frac{(1-f_{\bm{p}-\bm{q}}) M_{\bm{q}\nu}}{\Delta^{\text{virt}}_{\bm{q}\nu}}\right)^2\right) \\
    & = \exp\left(\left[\frac{\partial \Sigma^c(\bm{p},\omega)}{\partial \omega}\right]_{\omega = \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{HF}}\right)
\end{align}
and
\begin{align}
    \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP} & = \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{HF} - \left(\sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ}}\Delta^{\text{occ}}_{\bm{q}\nu} + \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{virt}}\Delta^{\text{virt}}_{\bm{q}\nu}\right) \\
    & = \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{HF} - \left(\sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \frac{f_{\bm{p}-\bm{q}} M_{\bm{q}\nu}^2}{\Delta^{\text{occ}}_{\bm{q}\nu}} + \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \frac{(1-f_{\bm{p}-\bm{q}}) M_{\bm{q}\nu}^2}{\Delta^{\text{virt}}_{\bm{q}\nu}}\right) \\
    & = \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{HF} + \Sigma^c(\bm{p},\epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{HF})
\end{align}
where we have used the fact that $\Sigma^c(\bm{p},\omega) = \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \frac{f_{\bm{p}-\bm{q}} M_{\bm{q}\nu}^2}{\omega + \Omega_\nu(\bm{q}) - \epsilon_{\bm{p}-\bm{q}}} + \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \frac{(1-f_{\bm{p}-\bm{q}}) M_{\bm{q}\nu}^2}{\omega - \Omega_\nu(\bm{q}) - \epsilon_{\bm{p}-\bm{q}}} \implies \left[\frac{\partial \Sigma^c(\bm{p},\omega)}{\partial \omega}\right]_{\omega = \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{HF}} = -\sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \left(\frac{f_{\bm{p}-\bm{q}} M_{\bm{q}\nu}}{\Delta^{\text{occ}}_{\bm{q}\nu}}\right)^2 - \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \left(\frac{(1-f_{\bm{p}-\bm{q}}) M_{\bm{q}\nu}}{\Delta^{\text{virt}}_{\bm{q}\nu}}\right)^2$.
\end{tcolorbox}
Next, we want to perform a Fourier transform.
\begin{align}
G^{GW+C}(\bm{p}, \omega) & = \int_{-\infty}^{\infty} dt\, e^{i \omega t} G^{GW+C}(\bm{p}, t) \\
& = -i Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP} \int_0^{\infty} dt\, e^{i(\omega - \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP}) t} e^{\sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ}} e^{-i\Delta^{\text{occ}}_{\bm{q}\nu} t} + \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{virt}} e^{-i\Delta^{\text{virt}}_{\bm{q}\nu} t}} \\
& = -i Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP} \int_0^{\infty} dt\, e^{i(\omega - \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP}) t} \left(1 + \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ}} e^{-i\Delta^{\text{occ}}_{\bm{q}\nu} t} + \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{virt}} e^{-i\Delta^{\text{virt}}_{\bm{q}\nu} t} + \ldots \right) \\
&= -i Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP} \int_0^{\infty} dt\, e^{[-\eta + i(\omega - \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP}) t]}\label{Gpp3} \\
&\quad - i Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP} \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ}} \int_0^{\infty} dt\, e^{[-\eta + i(\omega - \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP} - \Delta^{\text{occ}}_{\bm{q}\nu}) t]} \notag \\
&\quad - i Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP} \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{virt}} \int_0^{\infty} dt\, e^{[-\eta + i(\omega - \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP} - \Delta^{\text{virt}}_{\bm{q}\nu}) t]} + \ldots \notag \\
&= \frac{Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP}}{\omega - \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP} + i\eta} + \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \frac{Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ}}}{\omega - \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP} - \Delta^{\text{occ}}_{\bm{q}\nu} + i\eta} + \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \frac{Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{virt}}}{\omega - \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP} - \Delta^{\text{virt}}_{\bm{q}\nu} + i\eta} + \ldots\\
& = \frac{Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP}}{\omega - \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP} + i\eta} + \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \frac{Z_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}}}{\omega - \epsilon_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}} + i\eta} + \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \frac{Z_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{virt-sat}}}{\omega - \epsilon_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{virt-sat}} + i\eta} + \ldots
\end{align}
where we define the satellite energies $\epsilon_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}} = \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP} + \Delta^{\text{occ}}_{\bm{q}\nu}$ and $\epsilon_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{virt-sat}} = \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP} + \Delta^{\text{virt}}_{\bm{q}\nu}$, as well as the satellite weights $Z_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}} = Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ}}$ and $Z_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{virt-sat}} = Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP} \zeta_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{virt}}$.
\subsection{Spectral function}
The spectral function is obtained as (the virtual satellites spectral function, whose derivation will mirror that of the occupied satellites, are omitted for the sake of brevity)
\begin{align}
A^{GW+C}(\bm{p}, \omega) & = -\frac{1}{\pi} \operatorname{Im} G^{GW+C}(\bm{p}, \omega) \\
& = -\frac{1}{\pi} \operatorname{Im}\left[\frac{Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP}}{\omega - \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP} + i\eta} + \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \frac{Z_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}}}{\omega - \epsilon_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}} + i\eta} + \ldots \right] \\
& = -\frac{1}{\pi} \operatorname{Im}\left[\frac{\operatorname{Re} Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP} + i \operatorname{Im} Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP}}{\omega - \operatorname{Re} \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP} + i\left(\eta - \operatorname{Im} \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP}\right)} + \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \frac{\operatorname{Re} Z_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}} + i \operatorname{Im} Z_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}}}{\omega - \operatorname{Re} \epsilon_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}} + i\left(\eta - \operatorname{Im} \epsilon_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}}\right)} + \ldots \right] \\
& = -\frac{1}{\pi} \operatorname{Im}\left[\frac{\left(\operatorname{Re} Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP} + i \operatorname{Im} Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP}\right)\left(\omega - \operatorname{Re} \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP} - i\left(\eta - \operatorname{Im} \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP}\right)\right)}{\left(\omega - \operatorname{Re} \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP}\right)^2 + \left(\operatorname{Im} \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP}\right)^2}\right.\\
& \left. + \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \frac{\left(\operatorname{Re} Z_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}} + i \operatorname{Im} Z_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}}\right)\left(\omega - \operatorname{Re} \epsilon_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}} - i\left(\eta - \operatorname{Im} \epsilon_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}}\right)\right)}{\left(\omega - \operatorname{Re} \epsilon_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}}\right)^2 + \left(\operatorname{Im} \epsilon_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}}\right)^2} + \ldots \right] \\
& = -\frac{1}{\pi}\left[\frac{\left(\operatorname{Re} Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP}\right)\left(\operatorname{Im} \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP}\right) + \left(\operatorname{Im} Z_{\bm{p}}^{QP}\right)\left(\omega - \operatorname{Re} \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP}\right)}{\left(\omega - \operatorname{Re} \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP}\right)^2 + \left(\operatorname{Im} \epsilon_{\bm{p}}^{QP}\right)^2}\right.\\
& \left. + \sum_{\bm{q}\nu} \frac{\left(\operatorname{Re} Z_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}}\right)\left(\operatorname{Im} \epsilon_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}}\right) + \left(\operatorname{Im} Z_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}}\right)\left(\omega - \operatorname{Re} \epsilon_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}}\right)}{\left(\omega - \operatorname{Re} \epsilon_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}}\right)^2 + \left(\operatorname{Im} \epsilon_{\bm{p}\bm{q}\nu}^{\text{occ-sat}}\right)^2} + \ldots \right]
\end{align}
\section{LDA}
Our presentation follows the restricted formalism where two electrons of opposite spin occupy each spatial orbital.
\subsection{Analytic gradients}

We first recall the definition of the density in terms of $P_{\mu\nu}$ and the basis functions $\phi_\mu(\mathbf r)$:
\begin{align}
  \rho(\mathbf r)
  &= \frac{1}{V}\sum_{\mu\nu} P_{\mu\nu}
      \operatorname{Re}\!\left[\phi_\mu^*(\mathbf r)\phi_\nu(\mathbf r)\right].
\end{align}
Defining the real overlap factor
\begin{align}
  S_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf r)
  &= \operatorname{Re}\!\left[\phi_\mu^*(\mathbf r)\phi_\nu(\mathbf r)\right],
\end{align}
the derivative of $\rho(\mathbf r)$ with respect to a density-matrix
element is
\begin{align}
  \frac{\partial \rho(\mathbf r)}{\partial P_{\alpha\beta}}
  &= \frac{1}{V} S_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf r)
   = \frac{1}{V}\operatorname{Re}\!\left[\phi_\alpha^*(\mathbf r)\phi_\beta(\mathbf r)\right].
\end{align}
\subsubsection{Kinetic}
The kinetic energy is given by
\begin{align}
  E_k
  &= \sum_{\mu\nu} P_{\mu\nu} T_{\mu\nu}
\end{align}
\subsubsection{Hartree}
The Hartree energy is given by
\begin{align}
  E_H
  &= \frac{1}{2}\int d\mathbf r\; v_H(\mathbf r)\,\rho(\mathbf r) \\
\end{align}
\subsubsection{Exchange}
The LDA defines the exchange energy for a single particle as $\epsilon_x(\mathbf r) = -\frac{3}{4}\left(\frac{3}{\pi}\right)^{1/3} \rho(\mathbf r)^{1/3}$. So the total exchange energy becomes 
\begin{align}
  E_x[\rho]
  &= \int d\mathbf r\; \epsilon_x(\mathbf r)\,\rho(\mathbf r) \\
% &= \int d\mathbf r\; C \rho(\mathbf r)^{4/3} \\
  &= -\frac{3}{4}\left(\frac{3}{\pi}\right)^{1/3} \int d\mathbf r\; \rho(\mathbf r)^{4/3} \\
  &= -\frac{3}{4}\left(\frac{3}{\pi}\right)^{1/3} \int d\mathbf r\;
     \left(
       \frac{1}{V}\sum_{\mu\nu}P_{\mu\nu}S_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf r)
     \right)^{4/3} \\
&\approx -\frac{3}{4}\left(\frac{3}{\pi}\right)^{1/3} \frac{1}{V^{4/3}} \sum_g w_g
      \left(
        \sum_{\mu\nu}P_{\mu\nu}S_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf r_g)
      \right)^{4/3}.
\end{align}
Taking the derivative with respect to $P_{\alpha\beta}$ gives
\begin{align}
  \frac{\partial E_x}{\partial P_{\alpha\beta}}
&= -\frac{3}{4}\left(\frac{3}{\pi}\right)^{1/3} \int d\mathbf r\;
     \frac{4}{3}
     \left(
       \frac{1}{V}\sum_{\mu\nu}P_{\mu\nu}S_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf r)
     \right)^{1/3}
     \frac{\partial}{\partial P_{\alpha\beta}}
     \left(
       \frac{1}{V}\sum_{\mu\nu}P_{\mu\nu}S_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf r)
     \right) \\
   &= -\left(\frac{3}{\pi}\right)^{1/3} \int d\mathbf r\;
      \left(
        \frac{1}{V}\sum_{\mu\nu}P_{\mu\nu}S_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf r)
      \right)^{1/3}
      \left(
        \frac{1}{V} S_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf r)
      \right) \\
   &= -\left(\frac{3}{\pi}\right)^{1/3} \frac{1}{V^{4/3}} \int d\mathbf r\;
      \left(
        \sum_{\mu\nu}P_{\mu\nu}S_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf r)
      \right)^{1/3}
      S_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf r) \\
&\approx -\left(\frac{3}{\pi}\right)^{1/3} \frac{1}{V^{4/3}} \sum_g w_g
      \left(
        \sum_{\mu\nu}P_{\mu\nu}S_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf r_g)
      \right)^{1/3}
      S_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf r_g).
%   &= -\frac{9}{4\pi} \int d\mathbf r\;
%      \frac{4}{3}
%      \left(
%        \frac{1}{V}\sum_{\mu\nu}P_{\mu\nu}S_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf r)
%      \right)^{1/3}
%      \frac{\partial}{\partial P_{\alpha\beta}}
%      \left(
%        \frac{1}{V}\sum_{\mu\nu}P_{\mu\nu}S_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf r)
%      \right) \\
%   &= -\frac{3}{\pi} \int d\mathbf r\;
%      \left(
%        \frac{1}{V}\sum_{\mu\nu}P_{\mu\nu}S_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf r)
%      \right)^{1/3}
%      \left(
%        \frac{1}{V} S_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf r)
%      \right) \\
%   &= {-\frac{3}{\pi V^{4/3}} \int d\mathbf r\;
%      \left(
%        \sum_{\mu\nu}P_{\mu\nu}S_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf r)
%      \right)^{1/3}
%      S_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf r)}.
\end{align}

\subsubsection{Correlation}
Perdew defines the correlation energy as
\begin{align}
  E_c[\rho]
  &= \int d\mathbf r\; \epsilon _c(\mathbf r)\,\rho(\mathbf r),
\end{align}
with
\begin{align}
  \epsilon_c(\mathbf r)
  &= \frac{\gamma}{1 + \beta_1 \sqrt{r_s(\mathbf r)} + \beta_2 r_s(\mathbf r)},\\
  r_s(\mathbf r)
  &= \left(\frac{3}{4\pi\rho(\mathbf r)}\right)^{1/3}.
\end{align}
Then
\begin{align}
  \frac{\delta E_c}{\delta\rho(\mathbf r)}
  &= \epsilon_c(\mathbf r) + \rho(\mathbf r)\frac{d\epsilon_c}{d\rho}\Big|_{\rho(\mathbf r)} \\
  &= v_c(\mathbf r)
\end{align}
with $  v_c(\mathbf r) = \epsilon_c(\mathbf r)
     \left(
       1 + \frac{7}{6}\beta_1 \sqrt{r_s(\mathbf r)}
         + \frac{4}{3}\beta_2 r_s(\mathbf r)
     \right)$. To obtain the gradient with respect to the density-matrix element
$P_{\alpha\beta}$, we need:
\begin{align}
  \frac{\partial E_c}{\partial P_{\alpha\beta}}
  &= \frac{\partial E_c}{\partial \rho(\mathbf r)} \frac{\partial \rho(\mathbf r)}{\partial P_{\alpha\beta}} \\
  &= \int d\mathbf r\; v_c(\mathbf r) \frac{\partial \rho(\mathbf r)}{\partial P_{\alpha\beta}} \\
  &= \int d\mathbf r\; v_c(\mathbf r) \frac{1}{V} S_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf r) \\
&\approx \frac{1}{V} \sum_g w_g v_c(\mathbf r_g) S_{\alpha\beta}(\mathbf r_g).
\end{align}
\subsection{Fock matrix}
\subsubsection{Density operator}
I construct a real space cubic grid for the evaluation of my quantities on, which defines a set of grid points $\{\mathbf r_g\}$ with associated weights $\{w_g\}$. The density is then given by
\begin{align}
{\rho}(\mathbf r) = \sum_{\mu\nu} P_{\mu\nu} \phi_\mu^*(\mathbf r) \phi_\nu(\mathbf r) = \frac{1}{V} \sum_{\mu\nu} P_{\mu\nu} 
\end{align}

\section{Applying the Dyson density matrix to the electron gas }

\subsubsection{Obtaining the quasar boson quantities }
For each method, we need quasibosons frequencies, and fermionic couplings. For GW, These can be obtained from the excitation energies and transition densities taken from a Casida diagonalization of the dRPA problem. \emph{What would be their specific forms? }
For PT2 methods, these are The single-particle energy differences and the relevant ERIs between the occupied and virtual stattes. The best way to understand how these quantities are obtained is by considering the cumulant formulation for each method. Consider that for BWs2, I wrote down
\begin{align}
\Sigma_{\mathrm{eff},pp}(\omega + \tilde{\varepsilon}_p) &=
\Sigma_{pp}^{(2),2p1h+2p1h}\!\left[ G^{(0)'} \right](\omega + \tilde{\varepsilon}_p)
- \Sigma_{pp}^{(2),2h1p}\!\left[ G^{(0)'} \right](\tilde{\varepsilon}_p)\\
&= \frac{1}{2} \sum_{ija} \langle p a \| i j\rangle^2 \left( \frac{1}{\omega + \tilde{\varepsilon}_p+ \tilde{\varepsilon}_a  - \tilde{\varepsilon}_i - \tilde{\varepsilon}_j +i\eta} - \frac{1}{\tilde{\varepsilon}_p  + \tilde{\varepsilon}_a - \tilde{\varepsilon}_i - \tilde{\varepsilon}_j +i\eta} \right)\\
&\quad + \frac{1}{2} \sum_{iab} \frac{\langle p i \| a b\rangle^2}{\omega + \tilde{\varepsilon}_p + \tilde{\varepsilon}_i - \tilde{\varepsilon}_a - \tilde{\varepsilon}_b +i\eta} \\
&= \frac{1}{2} \sum_{ija} \langle p a \| i j\rangle^2 \frac{\omega}{(\omega - \tilde\epsilon_{pa}^{ij})(\tilde\epsilon_{pa}^{ij})} + \frac{1}{2} \sum_{iab} \frac{\langle p i \| a b\rangle^2}{\omega - \tilde\epsilon_{pi}^{ab}}
% \implies \operatorname{Im} \Sigma_{\mathrm{eff},pp}(\omega + \tilde{\varepsilon}_p) &= -\frac{1}{2} \sum_{ija} \langle p a \| i j\rangle^2 \frac{\omega \eta}{(\omega + \tilde\Delta_{pa}^{ij})^2 + \eta^2} + \frac{1}{2} \sum_{iab} \langle p i \| a b\rangle^2 \frac{\eta}{(\omega + \tilde\Delta_{pi}^{ab})^2 + \eta^2}
\end{align}
where we identify $\tilde{\epsilon_{pi}^{ab}} = \tilde{\varepsilon}_a + \tilde{\varepsilon}_b - \tilde{\varepsilon}_p - \tilde{\varepsilon}_i-i\eta$ and $\tilde{\epsilon}_{pa}^{ij} = \tilde{\varepsilon}_i + \tilde{\varepsilon}_j - \tilde{\varepsilon}_p - \tilde{\varepsilon}_a - i\eta$
as the bosonic frequencies in the quasi-boson approximation due to the BWs2 partition. I imagine that we should get something similar if we just do MP2. \emph{Can you write that down as well? }

\section{Madelung contributions to the self-energy}
From here on out, quantities colored red include a Madelung contribution, while quantities colored green do not.
We begin from
\begin{align}
\red{\hat H}
&=
\sum_{\mathbf k}\red{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{(0)}}\hat a_{\mathbf k}^\dagger \hat a_{\mathbf k}
+
\underbrace{\frac{1}{2\Omega}
\sum_{\mathbf q\neq\mathbf 0}v_q
\sum_{\mathbf k\mathbf k'}
a_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf{k}'-\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf k'}
a_{\mathbf k}}_{\green{\hat{V}}},
\end{align}
where $\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}'$ run over all valid momenta, and $\mathbf{q}$ runs over all nonzero momentum transfers. $\red{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{(0)}}=\frac{k^2}{2}+\frac{v_M}{2}$ is the bare single-particle energy with $v_M=-2.837297 \times\left(\frac{3}{4 \pi}\right)^{1 / 3} N^{-1 / 3} r_s^{-1}$ the Madelung constant for the cubic simulation cell. We will now normal order $\green{\hat{V}}$ with respect to
the HF reference $|\Phi_0\rangle$. Wick's theorem, which says that
$
a^\dagger a^\dagger a a
=
:a^\dagger a^\dagger a a:
+
\sum_{\text{single contractions}}
(\text{contraction})
:\!a^\dagger a\!:
+
\sum_{\text{complete contractions}}
(\text{contractions})$, gives
\begin{align}
\implies&
a_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf{k}'-\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf k'}
a_{\mathbf k}\\
&=
:
a_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf{k}'-\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf k'}
a_{\mathbf k}
:
\nonumber\\
&\quad
+
\wick{
\c1 a_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf{k}'-\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf k'}
\c1 a_{\mathbf k}
}
:
a_{\mathbf{k}'-\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf k'}
:
-
\wick{
\c1 a_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf{k}'-\mathbf q}^\dagger
\c1 a_{\mathbf k'}
a_{\mathbf k}
}
:
a_{\mathbf{k}'-\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf k}
:
\nonumber\\
&\quad
-
\wick{
a_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf q}^\dagger
\c1 a_{\mathbf{k}'-\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf k'}
\c1 a_{\mathbf k}
}
:
a_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf k'}

a_{\mathbf k}
}

a_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf k}
:

&\quad
+
\wick{
\c1 a_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf q}^\dagger
\c2 a_{\mathbf{k}'-\mathbf q}^\dagger
\c2 a_{\mathbf k'}
\c1 a_{\mathbf k}
}
-

\c1 a_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf q}^\dagger
\c2 a_{\mathbf{k}'-\mathbf q}^\dagger
\c1 a_{\mathbf k'}
\c2 a_{\mathbf k}
}
\\
&=
:
a_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf{k}'-\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf k'}
a_{\mathbf k}
:
\nonumber\\
&\quad
+
{
\delta_{\mathbf q,\mathbf 0}
n_{\mathbf k}

a_{\mathbf{k}'-\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf k'}
:

\nonumber\\

+

-

\\[1ex]

\implies

&=

&=
\underbrace{\frac{1}{2\Omega}
\sum_{\mathbf q\neq\mathbf 0}v_q
\sum_{\mathbf k\mathbf k'}
:
a_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf{k}'-\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf k'}
a_{\mathbf k}
: }_{\green{\hat V_{\mathrm{res}}}}
\underbrace{-\frac{1}{\Omega}
\sum_{\mathbf k}
\sum_{\mathbf q\neq\mathbf 0}
v_q n_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf q}
:
a_{\mathbf k}^\dagger a_{\mathbf k}
:}_{\sum_{\mathbf k}\green{\Sigma_{\mathbf k}^{x}} :\hat n_{\mathbf k}:}
\underbrace{-\frac{1}{2\Omega}
\sum_{\mathbf k}
\sum_{\mathbf q\neq\mathbf 0}
v_q n_{\mathbf k}n_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf q}}_{\green{E_x}}
\\[1ex]
\implies
\red{\hat H}
&=
\sum_{\mathbf k}
\red{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{(0)}}
\left( n_{\mathbf k} + :a_{\mathbf k}^\dagger a_{\mathbf k}:
\right)
+
\green{E_{\rm x}}
+
\sum_{\mathbf k}
\green{\Sigma_{\mathbf k}^{x}}
:
\hat a_{\mathbf k}^\dagger \hat a_{\mathbf k}
:
+
\green{\hat V_{\mathrm{res}}}
\\
&\equiv
\red{E_{\rm HF}}
+
\underbrace{\sum_{\mathbf k}
\red{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{\rm HF}}
:
\hat a_{\mathbf k}^\dagger \hat a_{\mathbf k}
:}_{\red{\hat F}}
+
\green{\hat V_{\mathrm{res}}}
\\
\implies \red{\hat{H}_N}
&= \red{\hat H} - \red{E_{\rm HF}}.
\end{align}
with ${
\red{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{\rm HF}}
}
=
{
\red{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{(0)}}
+
\green{\Sigma_{\mathbf k}^{x}}
}
=
\green{\frac{k^2}{2}}
+
\red{\frac{v_M}{2}}
+ \green{\Sigma_{\mathbf k}^{x}}
$ and $\red{E_{\rm HF}} = \sum_{\mathbf k}
\red{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{(0)}}
n_{\mathbf k}
+
\green{E_{\rm x}}$. At this point, we can pursue two different partitionings.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item $\red{\hat{H}_N} = \underbrace{\red{\hat F}}_{\hat{H_0}} + \underbrace{\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}}_{\hat{V}}$ 
    \item $\red{\hat{H}_N} = \underbrace{\red{\hat F_M}}_{\hat{H_0}} + \underbrace{\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}+\red{\hat S}}_{\hat{V}}$
\end{enumerate}
Note that $\red{\hat F_M} = \sum_{\mathbf k}\left(\green{\frac{k^2}{2}} + \green{\Sigma_{\mathbf k}^{x}}+\red{v_M n_{\mathbf k}}\right) :\hat a_{\mathbf k}^\dagger \hat a_{\mathbf k}:$ and 
\begin{align}
    \red{\hat S} &= \red{\hat F} - \red{\hat F_M}\\
&= \red{\frac{v_M}{2}} \sum_{\mathbf{k}}\left(1-2 n_{\mathbf{k}}\right) :a_{\mathbf{k}}^\dagger a_{\mathbf{k}}:
\end{align} 
\subsection{Answering the question of RPA screening}
\subsubsection{First partitioning }
%  We can rewrite our normal-ordered Hamiltonian as 
% \begin{equation}
%     \hat H_N
%     =
%     \underbrace{\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\left(
%     \green{\frac{k^2}{2}} - \frac{1}{\Omega}
%     \sum_{\mathbf{q}\neq\mathbf{0}} v_q n_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}
%     \right) :\hat a_{\mathbf{k}}^\dagger \hat a_{\mathbf{k}}:
%     +
%     \green{\hat V_{\mathrm{res}}}}_{\green{\hat H_N'}}
%     +
%      \frac{v_M}{2} \hat N,
% \end{equation}
% where we defined $\hat N \equiv \sum_{\mathbf{k}} :\hat a_{\mathbf{k}}^\dagger \hat a_{\mathbf{k}}:$.
Define $|\Phi_i^a\rangle = a_a^\dagger a_i|\Phi_0\rangle$. Then,
\begin{equation}
A_{ia,jb} = \langle\Phi_i^a|\red{\hat H_N}|\Phi_j^b\rangle
= \langle\Phi_i^a|\red{\hat F}|\Phi_j^b\rangle
+ \langle\Phi_i^a|\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}|\Phi_j^b\rangle ,
\end{equation}
with $\bm A\bm X=\bm X\bm\omega$, where $\bm\omega=\mathrm{diag}(\omega_n)$,
$\omega_n=E_n^{\rm CIS}-\red{E_{\rm HF}}$, and
$\sum_{ia}\langle\Phi_j^b|\red{\hat H}|\Phi_i^a\rangle\, c^{a}_{i,n}=E_n^{\rm CIS}\, c^{b}_{j,n}$. We know that the orbital energy differences come from 
\begin{align}
\langle\Phi_i^a|\red{\hat F}|\Phi_j^b\rangle
&= \delta_{ij}\delta_{ab}\left(\red{\epsilon_a^{\rm HF}}-\red{\epsilon_i^{\rm HF}}\right)\\
&= \delta_{ij}\delta_{ab}\left[\green{\frac{a^2-i^2}{2}}
+ \green{\Sigma^x_a} - \green{\Sigma^x_i} + \red{\frac{v_M}{2}} - \red{\frac{v_M}{2}}\right]\\
&= \delta_{ij}\delta_{ab}\left[\green{\frac{a^2-i^2}{2}}
+ \green{\Sigma^x_a} - \green{\Sigma^x_i}\right]\\
&= \langle\Phi_i^a|\green{\hat F}|\Phi_j^b\rangle,
\end{align}
with $\green{\hat F} = \sum_{\mathbf{k}}\green{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{\rm HF}} :\hat a_{\mathbf{k}}^\dagger \hat a_{\mathbf{k}}:$ and $\green{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{\rm HF}} = \green{\frac{k^2}{2}} + \green{\Sigma_{\mathbf k}^{x}}$.
\subsubsection{Second partitioning}
Now,
\begin{align}
A_{ia,jb}
&= \langle\Phi_i^a|\red{\hat F_M}|\Phi_j^b\rangle
+ \langle\Phi_i^a|\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}|\Phi_j^b\rangle
+ \langle\Phi_i^a|\red{\hat S}|\Phi_j^b\rangle \\
&= \delta_{ij}\delta_{ab}\left[\green{\frac{a^2}{2}}+\green{\Sigma^x_a}-\left(\green{\frac{i^2}{2}}+\green{\Sigma^x_i}+\red{v_M}\right)\right]
+ \langle\Phi_i^a|\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}|\Phi_j^b\rangle
+ \delta_{ij}\delta_{ab}\,\red{v_M} \\
&= \delta_{ij}\delta_{ab}\left[\green{\frac{a^2-i^2}{2}}+\green{\Sigma^x_a}-\green{\Sigma^x_i}\right]
+ \langle\Phi_i^a|\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}|\Phi_j^b\rangle\\
&= \langle\Phi_i^a|\green{\hat F}|\Phi_j^b\rangle
+ \langle\Phi_i^a|\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}|\Phi_j^b\rangle.
 \end{align}
\subsection{Answering the question of the GW self-energy }
We take the non-interacting Green's function to be 
\begin{align}
    G_p^0(\omega)=\frac{n_p}{\omega-\tilde{\epsilon}_p-i \eta}+\frac{1-n_p}{\omega-\tilde{\epsilon}_p+i \eta},
\end{align}
where $\tilde{\epsilon}_p$ is the single-particle energy of the reference. We then solve the Casida equation of 
\begin{align}
    \left(\begin{array}{ll}
\mathrm{A} & \mathrm{~B} \\
\mathrm{~B} & \mathrm{~A}
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}{ll}
\mathrm{X} & \mathrm{Y} \\
\mathrm{Y} & \mathrm{X}
\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}{cc}
\mathrm{X} & -\mathrm{Y} \\
-\mathrm{Y} & \mathrm{X}
\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}{ll}
\Omega & 0 \\
0 & \Omega
\end{array}\right)
\end{align}
using these single-particle energies, since in the GW approximation, the non-interacting polarizability is written as $P_0=-iG_0G_0$. Then we form the correlation self-energy as 
\begin{align}
    \Sigma_{pp}^{c,G_0W_0}(\omega)&= \sum_{j \nu}\frac{W_{p j \nu} W_{p j \nu}}{\omega-\left(\epsilon_j-\Omega_\nu\right)-\mathrm{i} \eta} + \sum_{b \nu}\frac{W_{p b \nu} W_{p b \nu}}{\omega-\left(\epsilon_b+\Omega_\nu\right)+\mathrm{i} \eta},
% = & \sum_{j \nu}\left[W_{p j \nu} \frac{1}{\omega-\left(\epsilon_j-\Omega_\nu\right)-\mathrm{i} \eta} W_{q j \nu}\right]+ \sum_{b \nu}\left[W_{p b \nu} \frac{1}{\omega-\left(\epsilon_b+\Omega_\nu\right)+\mathrm{i} \eta} W_{q b \nu}\right],
\end{align}
where $W_{n p \nu}=\sqrt{2}\sum_{i a} V_{n p i a}(\mathbf{X}+\mathbf{Y})_{i a, \nu}$ with $V_{n p i a}=(n i|p a)$ in the direct approximation.
% $$
% \begin{align*}
% \Sigma_{p q}^{c, \mathrm{G}_0 \mathrm{~W}_0}(\omega)= & \sum_{j \nu}\left[W_{p j \nu} \frac{1}{\omega-\left(\epsilon_j-\Omega_\nu\right)-\mathrm{i} \eta} W_{q j \nu}\right]+ \\
% & \sum_{b \nu}\left[W_{p b \nu} \frac{1}{\omega-\left(\epsilon_b+\Omega_\nu\right)+\mathrm{i} \eta} W_{q b \nu}\right], \tag{15}
% \end{align*}
% $$
% where the screened interaction matrix element $W_{n j \nu}$ is defined using the RPA X, Y amplitudes
% $$
% \begin{equation*}
% W_{n p \nu}=\sum_{i a} V_{n p i a}(\mathbf{X}+\mathbf{Y})_{i a, \nu} . \tag{16}
% \end{equation*}
% $$
\subsubsection{First partitioning }
Here, $\tilde{\epsilon}_p=\green{\epsilon_p^{\rm HF}}+\red{\frac{v_M}{2}}$. The Casida equation has
\begin{align}
    A_{ia,jb} &= \delta_{ij}\delta_{ab}\left(\green{\epsilon_a^{\rm HF}}+\red{\frac{v_M}{2}}-\green{\epsilon_i^{\rm HF}}-\red{\frac{v_M}{2}}\right)+\langle\Phi_i^a|\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}|\Phi_j^b\rangle\\
    &= \delta_{ij}\delta_{ab}\left(\green{\epsilon_a^{\rm HF}}-\green{\epsilon_i^{\rm HF}}\right)+\langle\Phi_i^a|\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}|\Phi_j^b\rangle\\
B_{ia,jb} &= \langle\Phi_i^a|\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}|\Phi_j^b\rangle.
\end{align}
The correlation self-energy is then
\begin{align}
    \Sigma_{pp}^{c,G_0W_0}(\omega)&= \sum_{j \nu}\frac{\green{W_{p j \nu}} \green{W_{p j \nu}}}{\omega-\left(\green{\epsilon_j^{\rm HF}}+\red{\frac{v_M}{2}}-\green{\Omega_\nu}\right)-\mathrm{i} \eta} + \sum_{b \nu}\frac{\green{W_{p b \nu}} \green{W_{p b \nu}}}{\omega-\left(\green{\epsilon_b^{\rm HF}}+\red{\frac{v_M}{2}}+\green{\Omega_\nu}\right)+\mathrm{i} \eta}
\end{align}
with QP equation
\begin{align}
    E^{GW}_p &= \tilde{\epsilon}_p + \Sigma_{pp}^{c,G_0W_0}(E^{GW}_p) \\
    &= \green{\epsilon_p^{\rm HF}}+\red{\frac{v_M}{2}} + \Sigma_{pp}^{c,G_0W_0}(E^{GW}_p).
\end{align}
\subsubsection{Second partitioning}
Here, $\tilde{\epsilon}_p=\green{\epsilon_p^{\rm HF}}+\red{v_M}n_p$. The Casida equation has
\begin{align}
    A_{ia,jb} &= \delta_{ij}\delta_{ab}\left(\green{\epsilon_a^{\rm HF}}-\green{\epsilon_i^{\rm HF}}-\red{v_M}\right)+\langle\Phi_i^a|\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}|\Phi_j^b\rangle+\delta_{ij}\delta_{ab}\red{v_M}\\
    &= \delta_{ij}\delta_{ab}\left(\green{\epsilon_a^{\rm HF}}-\green{\epsilon_i^{\rm HF}}\right)+\langle\Phi_i^a|\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}|\Phi_j^b\rangle\\
B_{ia,jb} &= \langle\Phi_{ij}^{ab}|\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}+\red{S}|\Phi_0\rangle\\
&= \langle\Phi_{ij}^{ab}|\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}|\Phi_0\rangle\\
\end{align}
The correlation self-energy is then
\begin{align}
    \Sigma_{pp}^{c,G_0W_0}(\omega)&= \sum_{j \nu}\frac{\green{W_{p j \nu}} \green{W_{p j \nu}}}{\omega-\left(\green{\epsilon_j^{\rm HF}}+\red{v_M}-\green{\Omega_\nu}\right)-\mathrm{i} \eta} + \sum_{b \nu}\frac{\green{W_{p b \nu}} \green{W_{p b \nu}}}{\omega-\left(\green{\epsilon_b^{\rm HF}}+\green{\Omega_\nu}\right)+\mathrm{i} \eta}
\end{align}
with QP equation for occupied states $k$
\begin{align}
    E^{GW}_k &= \tilde{\epsilon}_k + \Sigma_{kk}^{c,G_0W_0}(E^{GW}_k) \\
    &= \green{\epsilon_k^{\rm HF}}+\red{v_M} + \Sigma_{kk}^{c,G_0W_0}(E^{GW}_k)
\end{align}
and for virtual states $c$
\begin{align}
    E^{GW}_c &= \tilde{\epsilon}_c + \Sigma_{cc}^{c,G_0W_0}(E^{GW}_c) \\
    &= \green{\epsilon_c^{\rm HF}} + \Sigma_{cc}^{c,G_0W_0}(E^{GW}_c).
\end{align}
\subsection{Answering the question of MP2}
\subsubsection{First partitioning }
We know $
E^{(1)} = \langle\Phi_0|\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}|\Phi_0\rangle = 0
$.
Then,
\begin{align}
\green{E^{(2)}} &= -\frac{1}{4}\sum_{ijab}\frac{\left|\langle \Phi_{ij}^{ab}|\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}|\Phi_0\rangle\right|^2}{\green{E_{ij\rightarrow ab}^{(0)}}} \\
&\overset{RHF}{=}-\sum_{ijab}\frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ab}}}{{\green{E_{ij\rightarrow ab}^{(0)}}}}
\end{align}
where $\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ab}} = \green{(ia|bj)\left(2(ia|bj)-(ib|aj)\right)}$,
$\red{\hat F}|\Phi_{ij}^{ab}\rangle = \red{E_{ij\rightarrow ab}^{(0)}}|\Phi_{ij}^{ab}\rangle$, and
\begin{align}
\green{E^{(0)}_{ij\to ab}}
&= \red{\epsilon_a^{\rm HF}}+\red{\epsilon_b^{\rm HF}}-\red{\epsilon_i^{\rm HF}}-\red{\epsilon_j^{\rm HF}} \\
&= \green{\epsilon_a^{\rm HF}}+\green{\epsilon_b^{\rm HF}}-\green{\epsilon_i^{\rm HF}}-\green{\epsilon_j^{\rm HF}} + 2\red{\frac{v_M}{2}} - 2\red{\frac{v_M}{2}} \\
&= \green{\epsilon_a^{\rm HF}}+\green{\epsilon_b^{\rm HF}}-\green{\epsilon_i^{\rm HF}}-\green{\epsilon_j^{\rm HF}}
\end{align}
\subsubsection{Second partitioning}
Because $\red{\hat S}$ is a normal-ordered, one-body operator, $\langle\Phi_0|\red{\hat S}|\Phi_0\rangle=0$ and $\langle\Phi_{ij}^{ab}|\red{\hat S}|\Phi_0\rangle=0$. So $
    E^{(1)} = \langle\Phi_0|\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}|\Phi_0\rangle + \langle\Phi_0|\red{\hat S}|\Phi_0\rangle = 0
$. Then,
\begin{align}
\red{{E}^{(2)}_M} &= -\frac{1}{4}\sum_{ijab}\frac{\left|\langle \Phi_{ij}^{ab}|\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}|\Phi_0\rangle\right|^2}{\red{\tilde{E}_{ij\rightarrow ab}^{(0)}}}\\
&\overset{RHF}{=} -\sum_{ijab}\frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ab}}}{{\red{\tilde{E}_{ij\rightarrow ab}^{(0)}}}}
\end{align}
with $\red{\hat F_M}|\Phi_{ij}^{ab}\rangle = \tilde{E}_{ij\rightarrow ab}^{(0)}|\Phi_{ij}^{ab}\rangle$ and
\begin{align}
\red{\tilde{E}^{(0)}_{ij\to ab}}
&= \green{\epsilon_a^{\rm HF}}+\green{\epsilon_b^{\rm HF}}-\left(\green{\epsilon_i^{\rm HF}}+\red{v_M}\right)-\left(\green{\epsilon_j^{\rm HF}}+\red{v_M}\right) \\
&= \green{\epsilon_a^{\rm HF}}+\green{\epsilon_b^{\rm HF}}-\green{\epsilon_i^{\rm HF}}-\green{\epsilon_j^{\rm HF}}-2\red{v_M}\\
&= \green{E^{(0)}_{ij\to ab}}-2\red{v_M}.
\end{align}
\subsection{Answering the question of the second-order self-energy}
It is known that the first iteration of BWs2 must satisfy the constraint $\operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{W}^{O}) - \operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{W}^{V}) = E^{(2)}_N$. Consider the definitions
\begin{align}
{W}^{O}_{ii} &\equiv \frac{1}{2}\bm{\Sigma}_{ii}^{(2),2p1h}[\hat F](\omega = \epsilon_i^{\rm HF}) = \frac{1}{2}\sum_{a b j} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ab}}}{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i + \epsilon^{\rm HF}_j - \epsilon^{\rm HF}_a - \epsilon^{\rm HF}_b}\\
{W}^{V}_{aa} &\equiv \frac{1}{2}\bm{\Sigma}_{aa}^{(2),2h1p}[\hat F](\omega = \epsilon^{\rm HF}_a)= \frac{1}{2}\sum_{i j b} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ab}}}{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a + \epsilon^{\rm HF}_b - \epsilon^{\rm HF}_i - \epsilon^{\rm HF}_j}.
\end{align}
\subsubsection{First partitioning }
\begin{align}
    \operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{W}^{O}) - \operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{W}^{V}) &= \frac{1}{2}\Sigma_{ii}^{(2),2p1h}[\red{\hat F}](\omega = \red{\epsilon_i^{\rm HF}}) - \frac{1}{2}\Sigma_{aa}^{(2),2h1p}[\red{\hat F}](\omega = \red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a})
\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i j a b} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ab}}}{\red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i} + \red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_j} - \red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} - \red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_b}} - \frac{1}{2}\sum_{i j a b} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ab}}}{\red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} + \red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_b} - \red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i} - \red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_j}}\\
&= \sum_{i j a b} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ab}}}{\red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i} + \red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_j} - \red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} - \red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_b}}\\
&= E^{(2)}\\
\implies \Sigma^{(2)}_{pp}[\red{F}](\omega) &= \sum_{ija} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ap}}}{\omega-\red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i} + \left( \red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} - \red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_j}
\right)} + \sum_{iab} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ip}^{ab}}}{\omega-\red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} - \left(\red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_b} - \red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i}\right)}\\
&= \sum_{ija} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ap}}}{\omega-\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i} + \left( \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} - \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_j}
\right)+ \frac{\red{|v_M|}}{2}} + \sum_{iab} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ip}^{ab}}}{\omega-\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} - \left(\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_b} - \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i}\right)+ \frac{\red{|v_M|}}{2}}
\end{align}
The quasiparticle equation reads 
\begin{align}
    E_p^{(2)} &= \red{\epsilon_p^{\rm HF}} + \Sigma^{(2)}_{pp}[\red{F}](\omega = E_p^{(2)})\\
&\approx \red{\epsilon_p^{\rm HF}} + Z_p[\red{F}]\Sigma^{(2)}_{pp}[\red{F}](\omega = \red{\epsilon_p^{\rm HF}})\\
&= \red{\epsilon_p^{\rm HF}} + \left(1 - \frac{\partial \Sigma^{(2)}_{pp}[\green{F}](\omega)}{\partial \omega}\Big|_{\omega = \green{\epsilon_p^{\rm HF}}}\right)^{-1}\Sigma^{(2)}_{pp}[\green{F}](\omega = \green{\epsilon_p^{\rm HF}})
\end{align}
where 
\begin{align}
    Z_p[\red{F}] &= \left(1 - \frac{\partial \Sigma^{(2)}_{pp}[\red{F}](\omega)}{\partial \omega}\Big|_{\omega = \red{\epsilon_p^{\rm HF}}}\right)^{-1}\\
&= \left(1 + \left[ \sum_{ija} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ap}}}{\left(\red{\epsilon_p^{\rm HF}}-\red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i} + \left( \red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} - \red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_j}\right)\right)^2} + \sum_{iab} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ip}^{ab}}}{\left(\red{\epsilon_p^{\rm HF}}-\red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} - \left(\red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_b} - \red{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i}\right)\right)^2}\right]\right)^{-1}\\
&= \left(1 + \left[ \sum_{ija} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ap}}}{\left(\green{\epsilon_p^{\rm HF}}-\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i} + \left( \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} - \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_j}\right)\right)^2} + \sum_{iab} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ip}^{ab}}}{\left(\green{\epsilon_p^{\rm HF}}-\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} - \left(\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_b} - \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i}\right)\right)^2}\right]\right)^{-1}.
\end{align}
\subsubsection{Second partitioning }
\begin{align}
    \operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{W}^{O}) - \operatorname{Tr}(\mathbf{W}^{V}) &= \frac{1}{2}\Sigma_{ii}^{(2),2p1h}[\red{\hat F_M}](\omega = \green{\epsilon_i^{\rm HF}}+\red{v_M}) - \frac{1}{2}\Sigma_{aa}^{(2),2h1p}[\red{\hat F_M}](\omega = \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a})\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i j a b} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ab}}}{\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i} + \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_j} - \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} - \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_b} + 2\red{v_M}} - \frac{1}{2}\sum_{i j a b} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ab}}}{\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} + \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_b} - \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i} - \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_j} - 2\red{v_M}}\\
&= \sum_{i j a b} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ab}}}{\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i} + \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_j} - \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} - \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_b} + 2\red{v_M}}\\
&= E^{(2)}_M\\
\implies \Sigma^{(2)}_{pp}[\red{F_M}](\omega) &= \sum_{ija} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ap}}}{\omega -\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i} + \left( \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} - \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_j}\right) - 2\red{v_M}} + \sum_{iab} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ip}^{ab}}}{\omega-\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} - \left(\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_b} - \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i}\right) + \red{v_M}}.
\end{align}
For holes, the quasiparticle equation reads (with $\red{S_{kk}} = \langle\Phi_k|\red{\hat S}|\Phi_k\rangle = \epsilon^{\rm HF}_k[\red{F}] - \epsilon^{\rm HF}_k[\red{F_M}] = -\red{\frac{{v_M}}{2}}$)
\begin{align}
    E_k^{(2)} &= \green{\epsilon_k^{\rm HF}} + \red{v_M} + \red{S_{kk}}+\Sigma^{(2)}_{kk}[\red{F_M}](\omega = E_k^{(2)})\\
&\approx \green{\epsilon_k^{\rm HF}} + \red{v_M} + Z_k[\red{F_M}]\left( -\red{\frac{v_M}{2}}+\Sigma^{(2)}_{kk}[\red{F_M}](\omega = \green{\epsilon_k^{\rm HF}} + \red{v_M})\right),
\end{align}
where
\begin{align}
    Z_k[\red{F_M}] &= \left(1 - \frac{\partial \Sigma^{(2)}_{kk}[\red{F_M}](\omega)}{\partial \omega}\Big|_{\omega = \green{\epsilon_k^{\rm HF}} + \red{v_M}}\right)^{-1}\\
&= \left(1 + \left[ \sum_{ija} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ap}}}{\left(\green{\epsilon_k^{\rm HF}} -\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i} + \left( \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} - \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_j}\right) - \red{v_M}\right)^2} + \sum_{iab} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ip}^{ab}}}{\left(\green{\epsilon_k^{\rm HF}}-\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} - \left(\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_b} - \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i}\right) + 2\red{v_M}\right)^2}\right]\right)^{-1}.
\end{align}
For particles, the quasiparticle equation reads (with $\red{S_{cc}} = \langle\Phi_c|\red{\hat S}|\Phi_c\rangle = \epsilon^{\rm HF}_c[\red{F}] - \epsilon^{\rm HF}_c[\red{F_M}] = +\red{\frac{{v_M}}{2}}$)
\begin{align}
    E_c^{(2)} &= \green{\epsilon_c^{\rm HF}} + \red{S_{cc}}+\Sigma^{(2)}_{cc}[\red{F_M}](\omega = E_c^{(2)})\\
&\approx \green{\epsilon_c^{\rm HF}} + Z_c[\red{F_M}]\left(+\red{\frac{v_M}{2}}+\Sigma^{(2)}_{cc}[\red{F_M}](\omega = \green{\epsilon_c^{\rm HF}})\right),
\end{align}
where
\begin{align}
    Z_c[\red{F_M}] &= \left(1 - \frac{\partial \Sigma^{(2)}_{cc}[\red{F_M}](\omega)}{\partial \omega}\Big|_{\omega = \green{\epsilon_c^{\rm HF}}}\right)^{-1}\\
&= \left(1 + \left[ \sum_{ija} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ap}}}{\left(\green{\epsilon_c^{\rm HF}}-\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i} + \left( \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} - \green{\epsilon^{HF}_j}-2\red{v_M}\right)\right)^2} + \sum_{iab} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ip}^{ab}}}{\left(\green{\epsilon_c^{\rm HF}}-\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_a} - \left(\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_b} - \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_i}\right) + \red{v_M}\right)^2}\right]\right)^{-1}.
\end{align}
\section{Updated treatment of Madelung in plots}
All throughout, we take $\eta_\Sigma = 0^+\approx \rm{1e-8 Hartree}$ and $\eta_G$ is the actual finite broadening parameter, which is 0.2 eV and 0.8 eV for 14e19o and 114e485o.
\subsection{What has not changed}
We should check that things are equivalent if we instead compute the spectral function as $A_k(\omega)=-\frac{1}{\pi}\operatorname{Im}G_k(\omega)$, but redefining the $v_q$ in $\red{\Sigma^x_k}$ to be $v_q = \begin{cases} \frac{4\pi}{q^2}, & q\neq 0\\ -\red{v_M}, & q=0\end{cases}$.
\subsubsection{LDA+G0W0}
It is known that LDA is a semi-local theory, so Madelung does not play any role in the construction of the correlation self-energy. Therefore, the formula that was used to get the spectral function was 
\begin{align}
    A_k(\omega)&=-\frac{1}{\pi}\operatorname{Im}G^R_k(\omega)\\
&=\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{|\operatorname{Im}\Sigma^c_k[\green{\hat F^{\rm LDA}}, \eta_\Sigma](\omega )| + \eta_G}{\left(\omega - \green{F^{\rm LDA}_k} - \left(\red{\Sigma^x_k} + \operatorname{Re}\Sigma^c_k[\green{\hat F^{\rm LDA}}, \eta_\Sigma](\omega)-\green{V^{xc}_k}\right)\right)^2 + \left(|\operatorname{Im}\Sigma^c_k[\green{\hat F^{\rm LDA}}, \eta_\Sigma](\omega)| + \eta_G\right)^2},
\end{align}
where $\green{F^{\rm LDA}_k} = \green{\frac{k^2}{2}} + \green{V^{xc}_k}$ and $\green{V^{xc}_k}$ is the non-dispersive exchange-correlation potential of the LDA. $\red{\Sigma^x_k}=-\frac{1}{\Omega}\sum_{\mathbf{q}}v_q n_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}$ with $v_q = \begin{cases} \frac{4\pi}{q^2}, & q\neq 0\\ -\red{v_M}, & q=0\end{cases}$ is the non-local exchange self-energy. Finally,
\begin{align}
    \Sigma^c_k[\green{\hat F^{\rm LDA}}, \eta_\Sigma](\omega) = \sum_{j \nu}\frac{\green{W_{k j \nu} W_{k j \nu}}}{\omega-\left(\green{F^{\rm LDA}_j}-\green{\Omega_\nu}\right)+\mathrm{i} \eta_\Sigma} + \sum_{b \nu}\frac{\green{W_{k b \nu} W_{k b \nu}}}{\omega-\left(\green{F^{\rm LDA}_b}+\green{\Omega_\nu}\right)+\mathrm{i} \eta_\Sigma},
\label{eq:Sigma_c_LDA}
\end{align}
where $\green{W_{n p \nu}}=\sqrt{2}\sum_{i a} \green{(np|ia)(\mathbf{X}+\mathbf{Y})_{i a, \nu}}$ in the direct approximation, and $\green{\Omega_\nu}$ are solutions of the Casida eigenproblem with 
\begin{align}
    \green{A_{ia,jb}} &= \delta_{ij}\delta_{ab}\left(\green{F^{\rm LDA}_a}-\green{F^{\rm LDA}_i}\right)+ \green{(ia|bj)} \\
&= \delta_{ij}\delta_{ab}\left(\green{\frac{a^2-i^2}{2}}\right) + \green{(ia|bj)}\\
    \green{B_{ia,jb}} &= \green{(ia|jb)}.
\end{align}
\subsubsection{LDA+G0W0+C}
Here we are solving for the spectral function as 
\begin{align}
    A_k(\omega) &= -\frac{1}{\pi}\operatorname{Im}G^R_k(\omega)\\
G^R_k(t) &= \red{G_{0,k}^R(t)} \green{e^{C_{k}^{R}(t)}}
\end{align}
with 
\begin{align}
    \red{G_{0,k}^R(t)} = -\mathrm{i}\theta(t)e^{-\mathrm{i}\left(\green{F^{\rm LDA}_k} + \red{\Sigma^x_k} - \green{V^{xc}_k}-i\eta_G\right)t}
\end{align}
and 
\begin{align}
    \green{C_{k}^{R}(t)} = +\frac{1}{\pi}\int d\omega \frac{|\operatorname{Im}\Sigma^c_k[\green{\hat F^{\rm LDA}}, \eta_\Sigma](\omega+\green{F^{\rm LDA}_k})}{\omega^2} \left(e^{-\mathrm{i}\omega t}+\mathrm{i}\omega t-1\right).
\end{align}
\subsection{What I was doing before }
I had $\red{F^{\rm HF}_k} = \green{\frac{k^2}{2}} + \green{\Sigma^x_k}+\red{v_M}n_k$ and $\green{\Sigma^x_k}=-\frac{1}{\Omega}\sum_{\mathbf{q}}v_q n_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}$ with $v_q = \begin{cases} \frac{4\pi}{q^2}, & q\neq 0\\ 0, & q=0\end{cases}$ as the non-local exchange self-energy.
\subsubsection{HF+G0W0}
\begin{align}
    A_k(\omega)&=-\frac{1}{\pi}\operatorname{Im}G^R_k(\omega)\\
&=\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{|\operatorname{Im}\Sigma^c_k[\red{\hat F^{\rm HF}}, \eta_\Sigma](\omega )| + \eta_G}{\left(\omega - \red{F^{\rm HF}_k} - \left( \operatorname{Re}\Sigma^c_k[\red{\hat F^{\rm HF}}, \eta_\Sigma](\omega)\right)\right)^2 + \left(|\operatorname{Im}\Sigma^c_k[\red{\hat F^{\rm HF}}, \eta_\Sigma](\omega)| + \eta_G\right)^2}.
\end{align}
\begin{align}
    \Sigma^c_k[\red{F^{\rm HF}}, \eta_\Sigma](\omega) = \sum_{j \nu}\frac{\red{W_{k j \nu} W_{k j \nu}}}{\omega-\left(\red{F^{\rm HF}_j}-\red{\Omega_\nu}\right)+\mathrm{i} \eta_\Sigma} + \sum_{b \nu}\frac{\red{W_{k b \nu} W_{k b \nu}}}{\omega-\left(\red{F^{\rm HF}_b}+\red{\Omega_\nu}\right)+\mathrm{i} \eta_\Sigma},
\end{align}
where $\red{W_{n p \nu}}=\sqrt{2}\sum_{i a} \green{(np|ia)}\red{(\mathbf{X}+\mathbf{Y})_{i a, \nu}}$ in the direct approximation, and $\red{\Omega_\nu}$ are solutions of the Casida eigenproblem with 
\begin{align}
    \red{A_{ia,jb}} &= \delta_{ij}\delta_{ab}\left(\red{F^{\rm HF}_a}-\red{F^{\rm HF}_i}\right)+ \green{(ia|bj)} \\
    \green{B_{ia,jb}} &= \green{(ia|jb)}.
\end{align}
