\subsubsection{BWs2+C}
\begin{align}
    A_p(\omega) &= -\frac{1}{\pi}\operatorname{Im}G_{pp}^{R,\mathrm{(2;\alpha_1)+C}}(\omega)\\
G_{pp}^{R,\mathrm{(2;\alpha_1)+C}}(t) &= -i \theta(t) e^{-i\tilde{\varepsilon}_p t} \exp\left\{C_{pp}^{(2;\alpha_1)}(t)\right\}\\
    &= -i \theta(t) e^{-i\red{F^{\rm HF}_p} t} \exp\left\{-i\underbrace{W_{pp}}_{\frac{\alpha_1}{2}\bm{\Sigma}_{pp}^{(2),2p1h}[G^{(0)'}](\omega=\tilde{\varepsilon}_p)}t+\sum_{i a b}\frac{\mathcal{K}_{pi}^{ab} }{\left(\tilde{\epsilon}_{p i}^{a b}\right)^2}\left(e^{-i \tilde{\epsilon}_{p i}^{a b} t} +i \left( 1-\alpha_1 /2\right) \tilde{\epsilon}_{p i}^{a b} t -1 \right) +\ldots \right\} \\
&= -i \theta(t) e^{-i \red{F^{\rm HF}_p} t} \exp\left\{\sum_{i a b}\frac{\mathcal{K}_{pi}^{ab} }{\left(\tilde{\epsilon}_{p i}^{a b}\right)^2}\left(e^{-i \tilde{\epsilon}_{p i}^{a b} t} +i \tilde{\epsilon}_{p i}^{a b} t -1 \right) +\sum_{i j a} \frac{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{pa}}{\left(\tilde{\epsilon}_{p a}^{i j}\right)^2} \left(e^{-i \tilde{\epsilon}_{p a}^{i j} t} +i \tilde{\epsilon}_{p a}^{i j} t -1 \right) \right\}
\end{align}
with 
\begin{equation}
    C_{pp}^{(2;\alpha_1)}(t) = 
    \sum_{i a b}\frac{\mathcal{K}_{pi}^{ab} }{\left(\tilde{\epsilon}_{p i}^{a b}\right)^2}\left(e^{-i \tilde{\epsilon}_{p i}^{a b} t} +i \left( 1-\alpha_1 /2\right) \tilde{\epsilon}_{p i}^{a b} t -1 \right) +\sum_{i j a} \frac{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{pa}}{\left(\tilde{\epsilon}_{p a}^{i j}\right)^2} \left(e^{-i \tilde{\epsilon}_{p a}^{i j} t} +i \tilde{\epsilon}_{p a}^{i j} t -1 \right)
\end{equation}
where $\tilde{\epsilon}_{p i}^{a b} = \tilde{\varepsilon}_a + \tilde{\varepsilon}_b - \tilde{\varepsilon}_i - \tilde{\varepsilon}_p$ and $\tilde{\epsilon}_{p a}^{i j} = \tilde{\varepsilon}_i + \tilde{\varepsilon}_j - \tilde{\varepsilon}_a - \tilde{\varepsilon}_p$.
\subsection{What I am doing now}
Now, I am taking 
$\red{F^{\rm HF}_k} = \green{\frac{k^2}{2}} + \green{\Sigma^x_k}+\red{\frac{v_M}{2}}=\green{\frac{k^2}{2}} + \green{\Sigma^x_k}-\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}}$ and $\green{\Sigma^x_k}=-\frac{1}{\Omega}\sum_{\mathbf{q}}v_q n_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}$ with $v_q = \begin{cases} \frac{4\pi}{q^2}, & q\neq 0\\ 0, & q=0\end{cases}$ as the non-local exchange self-energy.
\subsubsection{HF+G0W0}
I have ($\omega_{\text{p}} = \omega \mp \red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}}\implies \omega = \omega_{\text{p}} \pm \red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}}$, where $\omega_p$ and $\omega$ are the frequency variables on the physical spectral function for electron removal/addition and in the simulation cell, respectively. Note that the upper sign corresponds to electron removal, while the lower sign corresponds to electron addition.) 
\begin{align}
    A_k(\omega_p)&=-\frac{1}{\pi}\operatorname{Im}G^R_k(\omega)\\
&=\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{|\operatorname{Im}\Sigma^c_k[\red{\hat F^{\rm HF}}](\omega)| + \eta_G}{\left((\omega) - \red{F^{\rm HF}_k} - \left( \operatorname{Re}\Sigma^c_k[\red{\hat F^{\rm HF}}](\omega)\right)\right)^2 + \left(|\operatorname{Im}\Sigma^c_k[\red{\hat F^{\rm HF}}](\omega)| + \eta_G\right)^2}\\
&=\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{|\operatorname{Im}\Sigma^c_k[\red{\hat F^{\rm HF}}](\omega)| + \eta_G}{\left(\omega - \green{\frac{k^2}{2}} - \green{\Sigma^x_k} + \red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}} - \left( \operatorname{Re}\Sigma^c_k[\red{\hat F^{\rm HF}}](\omega)\right)\right)^2 + \left(|\operatorname{Im}\Sigma^c_k[\red{\hat F^{\rm HF}}](\omega)| + \eta_G\right)^2}
\end{align}
with
\begin{align}
    \Sigma^c_k[\red{F^{\rm HF}}](\omega) &= \sum_{j \nu}\frac{\green{W_{k j \nu} W_{k j \nu}}}{\omega-\left(\red{F^{\rm HF}_j}-\green{\Omega_\nu}\right)+\mathrm{i} \eta_G} + \sum_{b \nu}\frac{\green{W_{k b \nu} W_{k b \nu}}}{\omega-\left(\red{F^{\rm HF}_b}+\green{\Omega_\nu}\right)+\mathrm{i} \eta_G}\\
&=\sum_{j \nu}\frac{\green{W_{k j \nu} W_{k j \nu}}}{\omega+\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}}-\left(\green{\frac{j^2}{2}} + \green{\Sigma^x_j}\right) + \green{\Omega_\nu}+\mathrm{i} \eta_G} + \sum_{b \nu}\frac{\green{W_{k b \nu} W_{k b \nu}}}{\omega+\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}}-\left(\green{\frac{b^2}{2}} + \green{\Sigma^x_b}\right) - \green{\Omega_\nu}+\mathrm{i} \eta_G}.
\end{align}
For one-shot electron removal spectral functions, this is equivalent to 
\begin{align}
    A_k(\omega_{\text{p}})&=-\frac{1}{\pi}\operatorname{Im}G^R_k(\omega_{\text{p}}+\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}})\\
&=\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{|\operatorname{Im}\Sigma^c_k[\red{\hat F^{\rm HF}}](\omega_{\text{p}}+\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}})| + \eta_G}{\left((\omega_{\text{p}}+\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}}) - \red{F^{\rm HF}_k} - \left( \operatorname{Re}\Sigma^c_k[\red{\hat F^{\rm HF}}](\omega_{\text{p}}+\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}})\right)\right)^2 + \left(|\operatorname{Im}\Sigma^c_k[\red{\hat F^{\rm HF}}](\omega_{\text{p}}+\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}})| + \eta_G\right)^2}\\
&=\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{|\operatorname{Im}\Sigma^c_k[\red{\hat F^{\rm HF}}](\omega_{\text{p}}+\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}})| + \eta_G}{\left(\omega_p  - \green{\frac{k^2}{2}} - \green{\Sigma^x_k} + \red{|v_M|} - \left( \operatorname{Re}\Sigma^c_k[\red{\hat F^{\rm HF}}](\omega_{\text{p}}+\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}})\right)\right)^2 + \left(|\operatorname{Im}\Sigma^c_k[\red{\hat F^{\rm HF}}](\omega_{\text{p}}+\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}})| + \eta_G\right)^2}
\end{align} with
\begin{align}
    \Sigma^c_k[\red{F^{\rm HF}}, \eta_G](\omega_{\text{p}}+\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}}) &= \sum_{j \nu}\frac{\green{W_{k j \nu} W_{k j \nu}}}{\omega_{\text{p}}+\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}}-\left(\red{F^{\rm HF}_j}-\green{\Omega_\nu}\right)+\mathrm{i} \eta_G} + \sum_{b \nu}\frac{\green{W_{k b \nu} W_{k b \nu}}}{\omega_{\text{p}}+\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}}-\left(\red{F^{\rm HF}_b}+\green{\Omega_\nu}\right)+\mathrm{i} \eta_G}\\
&=\sum_{j \nu}\frac{\green{W_{k j \nu} W_{k j \nu}}}{\omega_{\text{p}}+\red{|v_M|}-\left(\green{\frac{j^2}{2}} + \green{\Sigma^x_j}\right) + \green{\Omega_\nu}+\mathrm{i} \eta_G} + \sum_{b \nu}\frac{\green{W_{k b \nu} W_{k b \nu}}}{\omega_{\text{p}}+\red{|v_M|}-\left(\green{\frac{b^2}{2}} + \green{\Sigma^x_b}\right) - \green{\Omega_\nu}+\mathrm{i} \eta_G}.
\end{align}
$\green{W_{n p \nu}}=\sqrt{2}\sum_{i a} \green{(np|ia)}\green{(\mathbf{X}+\mathbf{Y})_{i a, \nu}}$ in the direct approximation, and $\green{\Omega_\nu}$ are solutions of the Casida eigenproblem. To derive the form of these matrices, consider that 
\begin{align}
\red{\hat H}
&=
\sum_{\mathbf k}
\red{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{(0)}}
\left( n_{\mathbf k} + :a_{\mathbf k}^\dagger a_{\mathbf k}:
\right)
+
\green{E_{\rm x}}
+
\sum_{\mathbf k}
\green{\Sigma_{\mathbf k}^{x}}
:
\hat a_{\mathbf k}^\dagger \hat a_{\mathbf k}
:
+
\green{\hat V_{\mathrm{res}}}
\\
&\equiv
\red{E_{\rm HF}}
+
\underbrace{\sum_{\mathbf k}
\red{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{\rm HF}}
:
\hat a_{\mathbf k}^\dagger \hat a_{\mathbf k}
:}_{\red{\hat F}}
+
\green{\hat V_{\mathrm{res}}}
\\
\implies \red{\hat{H}_N}
&= \red{\hat H} - \red{E_{\rm HF}}.
\end{align}
with ${
\red{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{\rm HF}}
}
=
{
\red{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{(0)}}
+
\green{\Sigma_{\mathbf k}^{x}}
}
=
\green{\frac{k^2}{2}}
+
\red{\frac{v_M}{2}}
+ \green{\Sigma_{\mathbf k}^{x}}
$, $\red{E_{\rm HF}} = \sum_{\mathbf k}
\red{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{(0)}}
n_{\mathbf k}
+
\green{E_{\rm x}}$, and $\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}=\frac{1}{2\Omega}\sum_{\mathbf{k}_1\mathbf{k}_2\mathbf{q}\neq 0}v_q :\hat a_{\mathbf{k}_1+\mathbf{q}}^\dagger \hat a_{\mathbf{k}_2-\mathbf{q}}^\dagger \hat a_{\mathbf{k}_2} \hat a_{\mathbf{k}_1}:
$. Consider the partitioning $\red{\hat{H}_N} = \underbrace{\red{\hat F}}_{\hat{H_0}} + \underbrace{\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}}_{\hat{V}}$.
% $A_{\mu \nu}=\frac{\partial^2 E}{\partial \kappa_\mu^* \partial \kappa_\nu}=\left\langle\Phi_0\right|\left[E_\mu,\left[\hat{H}, E_\nu^{\dagger}\right]\right]\left|\Phi_0\right\rangle$
\begin{align}
A_{\mu \nu}&=\frac{\partial^2 E}{\partial \kappa_\mu^* \partial \kappa_\nu}\\
&=\left\langle\Phi_0\right|\left[E_\mu,\left[\red{\hat{H}_N}, E_\nu^{\dagger}\right]\right]\left|\Phi_0\right\rangle\\
&=\left\langle\Phi_0\right|\left[E_\mu,\left[\red{\hat{F}^{\rm HF}}, E_\nu^{\dagger}\right]\right]\left|\Phi_0\right\rangle+\left\langle\Phi_0\right|\left[E_\mu,\left[\green{\hat{V}_{\mathrm{res}}}, E_\nu^{\dagger}\right]\right]\left|\Phi_0\right\rangle\\
    \green{A_{ia,jb}} &= \delta_{ij}\delta_{ab}\left(\red{F^{\rm HF}_a}-\red{F^{\rm HF}_i}\right)+ \green{(ia|bj)} \\
    &= \delta_{ij}\delta_{ab}\left(\green{\frac{a^2}{2} + \green{\Sigma^x_a}}-\green{\frac{i^2}{2} - \green{\Sigma^x_i}}\right)+ \green{(ia|bj)}\\
    \green{B_{ia,jb}} &= \green{(ia|jb)}.
\end{align}
% \subsubsection{scGW0@HF}
% The retarded Green's function for the next iteration is $G^R_k(\omega)\equiv G^R_k(\omega_p \pm \red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}})$. The new correlation self-energy is
% \begin{equation}
%     \Sigma_i^R(t)=i \sum_j\left[G_j^R(t) W_{i j}^{0,>}(t)+G_j^{<}(t) W_{i j}^{0, R}(t)\right] .
% \end{equation}
\subsubsection{HF+G0W0+C}
Here I am solving for the spectral function as 
\begin{align}
    A_k(\omega-\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}}) &= -\frac{1}{\pi}\operatorname{Im}G^R_k(\omega)\\
G^R_k(t) &= \red{G_{0,k}^R(t)} \green{e^{C_{k}^{R}(t)}}
\end{align}
with 
\begin{align}
    \red{G_{0,k}^R(t)} = -\mathrm{i}\theta(t)e^{-\mathrm{i}\left(\red{F^{\rm HF}_k} - i\eta_G\right)t}
\end{align}
and 
\begin{align}
    \green{C_{k}^{R}(t)} = +\frac{1}{\pi}\int d\omega \frac{|\operatorname{Im}\Sigma^c_k[\red{\hat F^{\rm HF}}, \eta_\Sigma](\omega+\red{F^{\rm HF}_k})}{\omega^2} \left(e^{-\mathrm{i}\omega t}+\mathrm{i}\omega t-1\right).
\end{align}
The idea is to solve for the time-domain cumulant and the time-domain Green's function all in the time frame of the simulation cell, then Fourier transform into the frequency frame of the simulation cell, and only then shift to the frequency frame of the electron removal spectral function. 
\subsubsection{BWs2}
We have solved the non-interacting Hamiltonian as 
\begin{equation}
\mathbf{H}_0' = \begin{pmatrix} \red{\mathbf{F}_{oo}} + \alpha_1 \red{\mathbf{W}^{O}} & 0 \\ 0 & \red{\mathbf{F}_{vv}} + \alpha_2 \red{\mathbf{W}^{V}} \end{pmatrix},
\end{equation}
where
\begin{align}
\mathbf{W}^{O}_{ii} &= \frac{1}{2}\bm{\Sigma}_{ii}^{(2),2p1h}(\omega = \tilde{\varepsilon}_i)= \frac{1}{2}\sum_{a b j} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ab}}}{\tilde{\varepsilon}_i + \tilde{\varepsilon}_j - \tilde{\varepsilon}_a - \tilde{\varepsilon}_b},\\
\mathbf{W}^{V}_{aa} &= \frac{1}{2}\bm{\Sigma}_{aa}^{(2),2h1p}(\omega = \tilde{\varepsilon}_a)= \frac{1}{2}\sum_{i j b} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ab}}}{\tilde{\varepsilon}_a + \tilde{\varepsilon}_b - \tilde{\varepsilon}_i - \tilde{\varepsilon}_j},
\end{align}
where $\tilde{\varepsilon}$ is the BWs2 ground-state MO energy and we chose the starting point of the self-consistency cycle to be the HF MO energies due to $\red{F^{\rm HF}_k}$. Given that the idea is to define the noninteracting Green's function as
\begin{align}
G^{(0)'}(\omega)
&=
\big( \omega - (\mathbf{F} + \underbrace{\alpha_1 \mathbf{W}^O \oplus \alpha_2 \mathbf{W}^V}_{\mathbf{W}}) \big)^{-1}\\
\implies G^{-1}(\omega)
% &= \omega - \mathbf{F} - \bm{\Sigma}(\omega) \\
% &= \omega - (\mathbf{F} + \mathbf{W})
%    - \big[ \bm{\Sigma}(\omega) - \mathbf{W} \big] \\
&= \big( G^{(0)'}(\omega) \big)^{-1}
   - \bm{\Sigma}_{\mathrm{eff}}(\omega),
\end{align}
with $\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathrm{eff}}(\omega) = \bm{\Sigma}(\omega) - \mathbf{W} \approx \bm{\Sigma}^{(2)}[G^{(0)'}(\omega)] - \mathbf{W}$. The correlation self-energy is
\begin{align}
    \Sigma^{(2)}_{pp}[G^{(0)'}(\omega),\etaS](\omega) &= \sum_{ija} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{ap}}}{\omega-\tilde{\varepsilon}_i + \left( \tilde{\varepsilon}_a - \tilde{\varepsilon}_j\right)+\mathrm{i} \etaS} + \sum_{iab} \frac{\green{\mathcal{K}_{ip}^{ab}}}{\omega-\tilde{\varepsilon}_a - \left(\tilde{\varepsilon}_b - \tilde{\varepsilon}_i\right)+\mathrm{i} \etaS}.
\end{align}
 The spectral function is then
\begin{align}
    A_k(\omega-\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}}) &= -\frac{1}{\pi}\operatorname{Im}G^R_k(\omega)\\
&=\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{|\operatorname{Im}\Sigma^{(2)}_k[G^{(0)'}, \eta_\Sigma](\omega )| + \eta_G}{\left(\omega - \tilde{\varepsilon}_k -\left( \operatorname{Re}\Sigma^{(2)}_k[G^{(0)'}, \eta_\Sigma](\omega)-\mathbf{W}_{kk}\right)\right)^2 + \left(|\operatorname{Im}\Sigma^{(2)}_k[G^{(0)'}, \eta_\Sigma](\omega)| + \eta_G\right)^2}\\
&=\frac{1}{\pi} \frac{|\operatorname{Im}\Sigma^{(2)}_k[G^{(0)'}, \eta_\Sigma](\omega )| + \eta_G}{\left(\omega - \green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_k}+\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}} - \operatorname{Re}\Sigma^{(2)}_k[G^{(0)'}, \eta_\Sigma](\omega)\right)^2 + \left(|\operatorname{Im}\Sigma^{(2)}_k[G^{(0)'}, \eta_\Sigma](\omega)| + \eta_G\right)^2}.
\end{align}
\subsubsection{BWs2+C}
Here
\begin{align}
    A_p(\omega-\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}}    ) &= -\frac{1}{\pi}\operatorname{Im}G_{pp}^{R,\mathrm{(2;\alpha_1)+C}}(\omega)\\
G_{pp}^{R,\mathrm{(2;\alpha_1)+C}}(t) &= -i \theta(t) e^{-i\tilde{\varepsilon}_p t} \exp\left\{C_{pp}^{(2;\alpha_1)}(t)\right\}\\
    &= -i \theta(t) e^{-i(\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_p}-\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}}) t} \exp\left\{-i\underbrace{W_{pp}}_{\frac{\alpha_1}{2}\bm{\Sigma}_{pp}^{(2),2p1h}[G^{(0)'}](\omega=\tilde{\varepsilon}_p)}t+\sum_{i a b}\frac{\mathcal{K}_{pi}^{ab} }{\left(\tilde{\epsilon}_{p i}^{a b}\right)^2}\left(e^{-i \tilde{\epsilon}_{p i}^{a b} t} +i \left( 1-\alpha_1 /2\right) \tilde{\epsilon}_{p i}^{a b} t -1 \right) +\ldots \right\} \\
&= -i \theta(t) e^{-i (\green{\epsilon^{\rm HF}_p}-\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}}) t} \exp\left\{\sum_{i a b}\frac{\mathcal{K}_{pi}^{ab} }{\left(\tilde{\epsilon}_{p i}^{a b}\right)^2}\left(e^{-i \tilde{\epsilon}_{p i}^{a b} t} +i \tilde{\epsilon}_{p i}^{a b} t -1 \right) +\sum_{i j a} \frac{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{pa}}{\left(\tilde{\epsilon}_{p a}^{i j}\right)^2} \left(e^{-i \tilde{\epsilon}_{p a}^{i j} t} +i \tilde{\epsilon}_{p a}^{i j} t -1 \right) \right\}
\end{align}
with 
\begin{equation}
    C_{pp}^{(2;\alpha_1)}(t) = 
    \sum_{i a b}\frac{\mathcal{K}_{pi}^{ab} }{\left(\tilde{\epsilon}_{p i}^{a b}\right)^2}\left(e^{-i \tilde{\epsilon}_{p i}^{a b} t} +i \left( 1-\alpha_1 /2\right) \tilde{\epsilon}_{p i}^{a b} t -1 \right) +\sum_{i j a} \frac{\mathcal{K}_{ij}^{pa}}{\left(\tilde{\epsilon}_{p a}^{i j}\right)^2} \left(e^{-i \tilde{\epsilon}_{p a}^{i j} t} +i \tilde{\epsilon}_{p a}^{i j} t -1 \right)
\end{equation}
\subsection{Plot labels}
I use the definition $\green{\Sigma^x_k}=-\frac{1}{\Omega}\sum_{\mathbf{q}}v_q n_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}$ with $v_q = \begin{cases} \frac{4\pi}{q^2}, & q\neq 0\\ 0, & q=0\end{cases}$. $\red{F^{\rm HF}}$ is diagonal.
\subsubsection{Before }
Before, I had $\red{F^{\rm HF}_k} = \green{\frac{k^2}{2}} + \green{\Sigma^x_k}-\red{|v_M|}n_k$. The spectral function plotted is the spectral function obtained in the simulation cell. 
\subsubsection{Now}
Now, I am taking 
$\red{F^{\rm HF}_k} =\green{\frac{k^2}{2}} + \green{\Sigma^x_k}-\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}}$. The spectral function plotted is the spectral function obtained in the simulation cell \emph{shifted} towards higher binding energies by $\red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}}$.
\subsubsection{Why this new form for HF energies}
Partition the normal-ordered Hamiltonian as $\red{\hat{H}_N}=\red{F^{\rm HF}}+\green{\hat V_{\rm res}} $, where $\red{\hat{H}_N} = \red{\hat H} - \red{E_{\rm HF}}$. I derived 
\begin{align}
\red{\hat H}
&=
\sum_{\mathbf k}\red{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{(0)}}\hat a_{\mathbf k}^\dagger \hat a_{\mathbf k}
+
{\frac{1}{2\Omega}
\sum_{\mathbf q\neq\mathbf 0}v_q
\sum_{\mathbf k\mathbf k'}
a_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf{k}'-\mathbf q}^\dagger
a_{\mathbf k'}
a_{\mathbf k}}\\
& \vdots\\
&=
\sum_{\mathbf k}
\red{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{(0)}}
\left( n_{\mathbf k} + :a_{\mathbf k}^\dagger a_{\mathbf k}:
\right)
+
\green{E_{\rm x}}
+
\sum_{\mathbf k}
\green{\Sigma_{\mathbf k}^{x}}
:
\hat a_{\mathbf k}^\dagger \hat a_{\mathbf k}
:
+
\green{\hat V_{\mathrm{res}}}
\\
&=
\red{E_{\rm HF}}
+
\underbrace{\sum_{\mathbf k}
\red{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{\rm HF}}
:
\hat a_{\mathbf k}^\dagger \hat a_{\mathbf k}
:}_{\red{\hat F^{\rm HF}}}
+
\green{\hat V_{\mathrm{res}}}
\end{align}
where ${
\red{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{\rm HF}}
}
=
{
\red{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{(0)}}
+
\green{\Sigma_{\mathbf k}^{x}}
}
=
\green{\frac{k^2}{2}}
+
\red{\frac{v_M}{2}}
+ \green{\Sigma_{\mathbf k}^{x}}
$, $\red{E_{\rm HF}} = \sum_{\mathbf k}
\red{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{(0)}}
n_{\mathbf k}
+
\green{E_{\rm x}}$, and $\green{\hat V_{\rm res}}=\frac{1}{2\Omega}\sum_{\mathbf{k}_1\mathbf{k}_2\mathbf{q}\neq 0}v_q :\hat a_{\mathbf{k}_1+\mathbf{q}}^\dagger \hat a_{\mathbf{k}_2-\mathbf{q}}^\dagger \hat a_{\mathbf{k}_2} \hat a_{\mathbf{k}_1}:
$. We defined $\red{\epsilon_{\mathbf k}^{(0)}}=\green{\frac{k^2}{2}}+\red{\frac{v_M}{2}}$ as the bare single-particle energy with $\red{v_M}=-2.837297 \times\left(\frac{3}{4 \pi}\right)^{1 / 3} N^{-1 / 3} r_s^{-1}$ the Madelung constant for the cubic simulation cell.

\subsection{Appealing to the definition of IP and EA}
Define $
    \green{\Sigma^x_k} = -\frac{1}{\Omega}\sum_{\mathbf{q}\neq0} v_q\, n_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}$ and
$    \green{E_x} = -\frac{1}{2\Omega}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\sum_{\mathbf{q}\neq0} v_q\, n_{\mathbf{k}} n_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}
    = -\frac{1}{2\Omega}\sum_{\mathbf{k}\neq\mathbf{k}'} v_{\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{k}'}\, n_{\mathbf{k}} n_{\mathbf{k}'}
    = \frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\green{\Sigma^x_k}\, n_{\mathbf{k}}$.
We have
\begin{align}
    \red{E_{\rm HF}}(N) &= \sum_{\mathbf k} \green{\frac{k^2}{2}}\, n_k + \green{E_x} - \frac{N\red{|v_M|}}{2}  \\
    &= \sum_{\mathbf k} \left(\green{\frac{k^2}{2}} - \red{\frac{|v_M|}{2}}\right) n_k + \green{E_x} \label{eq:hf_energy}
\end{align}
\subsubsection{IP}
If we removed an electron from occupied spin-orbital $\mathbf{i}$, then
\begin{align}
    \rm IP &\equiv \red{E_{\rm HF}}(N-1) - \red{E_{\rm HF}}(N)\\
    &= -\left(\green{\frac{i^2}{2}} + \green{\Sigma^x_i}\right) + \frac{\red{|v_M|}}{2}\\
    &\equiv -\red{F^{\rm HF}_i},
\end{align}
since $
    \green{E_x}(N-1) = \green{E_x}(N) + \frac{1}{\Omega}\sum_{\mathbf{k}'\neq\mathbf{i}} v_{\mathbf{i}-\mathbf{k}'}\, n_{\mathbf{k}'}
    = \green{E_x}(N) - \green{\Sigma^x_i}$.
\subsubsection{EA}
Add an electron to unoccupied spin-orbital $\mathbf{j}$,
\begin{align}
    \rm EA &\equiv \red{E_{\rm HF}}(N+1) - \red{E_{\rm HF}}(N)\\
    &= \left(\green{\frac{j^2}{2}} + \green{\Sigma^x_j}\right) - \frac{\red{|v_M|}}{2}\\
    &= \red{F^{\rm HF}_j},
\end{align}
since $
    \green{E_x}(N+1) = \green{E_x}(N) - \frac{1}{\Omega}\sum_{\mathbf{k}'} v_{\mathbf{j}-\mathbf{k}'}\, n_{\mathbf{k}'}
    = \green{E_x}(N) + \green{\Sigma^x_j}$.


\subsection{Interpretation of the first part}
It is written \emph{The Madelung constant can be either included in the Hamiltonian as written in Eq.~(64) or it can be included as an a posteriori correction to the simulation done without it.} I interpret this as saying that it makes no difference whether the Madelung constant gets lumped into the single-particle energies, as in Eq.~(\ref{eq:hf_energy}), or added afterwards as
\begin{equation}
    \red{E_{\rm HF}}(N) + \frac{N\red{|v_M|}}{2} = \sum_{\mathbf k} \green{\frac{k^2}{2}}\, n_k + \green{E_x}. \label{eq:hf_energy_noM}
\end{equation}
 This is the meaning of \emph{when the latter choice is made, the spectral functions have to be shifted accordingly in order to compare the results obtained from the former approach.}

\subsection{Interpretation of the second part}
\emph{Regardless of whether we included the Madelung constant in the Hamiltonian, there is an additional correction of $-\xi/2$ for both IP and EA to remove spurious image interactions coming from the excess charge created.} This means
\begin{align}
    E_g &\equiv EA + \frac{\red{|v_M|}}{2} + IP + \frac{\red{|v_M|}}{2}\\
    &= \left(\green{\frac{j^2}{2}} + \green{\Sigma^x_j} - \frac{\red{|v_M|}}{2}\right) + \left[-\left(\green{\frac{i^2}{2}} + \green{\Sigma^x_i}\right) + \frac{\red{|v_M|}}{2}\right]+ \red{|v_M|}\\
&= \green{\frac{j^2}{2}} + \green{\Sigma^x_j} - \green{\frac{i^2}{2}} - \green{\Sigma^x_i} + \red{|v_M|}
\end{align}
which is consistent with what is done in QCPBC.
\subsection{What before does}
We know \textbf{before} gives correct band gaps. Also, it gives the correct HF total energy:
\begin{align}
    \red{E_{\rm HF}}(N) &= \sum_{\mathbf k} \green{\frac{k^2}{2}}\, n_k - \frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{k}} n_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\frac{1}{\Omega}\sum_{\mathbf{q}} v_q\, n_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}\right) \\
    &= \sum_{\mathbf k} \green{\frac{k^2}{2}}\, n_k - \frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{k}} n_{\mathbf{k}}\left(\frac{1}{\Omega}\left[\sum_{\mathbf{q}\neq 0} v_q\, n_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}} + \Omega\red{|v_M|}\, n_{\mathbf{k}}\right]\right) \\
    &= \sum_{\mathbf k} \green{\frac{k^2}{2}}\, n_k + \frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\green{\Sigma^x_k}\, n_{\mathbf{k}} - \frac{\red{|v_M|}}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{k}} n_{\mathbf{k}}^2 \\
    &= \sum_{\mathbf k} \green{\frac{k^2}{2}}\, n_k + \green{E_x} - \frac{N\red{|v_M|}}{2},
\end{align}
where the last step uses $n_{\mathbf{k}}^2 = n_{\mathbf{k}}$ for spin-orbital occupations. This is identical to \ref{eq:hf_energy}. The corresponding HF single-particle energies are
\begin{equation}
    \varepsilon^{\rm HF}_k = \green{\frac{k^2}{2}} + \green{\Sigma^x_k} - \red{|v_M|}\, n_{\mathbf{k}},
\end{equation}
 The eigenvalue gap is then $\left(\green{\frac{j^2}{2}} + \green{\Sigma^x_j}\right) - \left(\green{\frac{i^2}{2}} + \green{\Sigma^x_i}\right) + \red{|v_M|} \equiv E_g$, so \textbf{before} reaches the corrected gap directly, without going through the uncorrected IP and EA.

\begin{enumerate}
    \item The \textbf{before} scheme reproduces the total energy because only occupied $n_{\mathbf{k}}$ enter that sum, but a self-energy is evaluated on both occupied and unoccupied states. Is there a way to check this using Galitskii-Migdal?
\end{enumerate}
\section{Exact answers for the quasiboson Hamiltonian}
We can write the quasiboson Hamiltonian as
\begin{align}
\hat{H}
&=
\hat{H}_{\rm el}
+
\hat{H}_{\rm pl}
+
\hat{H}_{\rm ep}
\end{align}
\subsection{Just 2h1p}
Here 
\begin{align}
\hat{H}_{\rm el}
&=
  \sum_{\bm{k}\in\mathcal{O}}
\epsilon_{\bm{k}}^{\rm HF}
a_{\bm{k}}^{\dagger}a_{\bm{k}},
\end{align}
\begin{align}
    \hat{H}_{\rm pl} = \sum_{\nu_{\bm{q}}\in\mathcal{M}}
\omega_{\nu_{\bm{q}}}^{\rm pl}
b_{\nu_{\bm{q}}}^{\dagger}
b_{\nu_{\bm{q}}},
\end{align}
and
\begin{align}
    \hat{H}_{\rm ep} = \sum_{\bm{k},\bm{k}+\bm{q}\in\mathcal{O}}
\sum_{\nu_{\bm{q}}\in\mathcal{M}}
g_{\nu}^{\rm pl}(\bm{k},\bm{q})
a_{\bm{k}+\bm{q}}^{\dagger}
a_{\bm{k}}
\left(
b_{\nu_{-\bm{q}}}^{\dagger}
+
b_{\nu_{\bm{q}}}
\right)
\end{align}
\subsubsection{ph-RPA}
Here, the idea is to get the quasiboson values from dRPA@LDA.
\subsubsection{pp-RPA}
Here, the idea is to get the quasiboson values from ppRPA@LDA. The Casida equation is 
\begin{equation}
    \begin{pmatrix}
        A & B \\
        B^\dag & C
    \end{pmatrix}
    \begin{pmatrix}
        X \\
Y
    \end{pmatrix}
    =
    \Omega
    \begin{pmatrix}
        I & 0 \\
0 & -I
    \end{pmatrix}
    \begin{pmatrix}
        X \\
Y
    \end{pmatrix}
\end{equation}
with
\begin{align}
    A_{ab,cd} = \delta_{a c} \delta_{b d}\left(\epsilon_a+\epsilon_b\right) + \frac{1}{\sqrt{\left(1+\delta_{a b}\right)\left(1+\delta_{c d}\right)}}(\langle a b || c d\rangle+\langle a b || d c\rangle) \\
    B_{ab,kl} = \frac{1}{\sqrt{\left(1+\delta_{a b}\right)\left(1+\delta_{k l}\right)}}(\langle a b || k l\rangle+\langle a b || l k\rangle) \\
    C_{ij,kl} = -\delta_{i k} \delta_{j l}\left(\epsilon_i+\epsilon_j\right) + \frac{1}{\sqrt{\left(1+\delta_{i j}\right)\left(1+\delta_{k l}\right)}}(\langle i j || k l\rangle+\langle i j || l k\rangle)
\end{align}

% B_{a b, k l}^{\mathrm{s}} & =\frac{1}{\sqrt{\left(1+\delta_{a b}\right)\left(1+\delta_{k l}\right)}}(\langle a b \mid k l\rangle+\langle a b \mid l k\rangle) \\

\begin{align}

\omega_{\nu_{\bm{q}}}^{\rm pl}

a_{\bm{k}+\bm{q}}^{\dagger}

Note that $\Omega_\nu\equiv \omega_{\nu_q}^{\rm pl}$ and $\mathcal{M}_{pm\nu_q}\equiv g_{\nu}^{\rm pl}(p,q)$ for the plasmon mode $\nu$ with momentum $\mathbf{q}$, such that $m=p+\mathbf{q}$.
\section{Reduction to G0W0+C}
\subsubsection{Setup}
For an occupied orbital $p$,
\begin{align}
G_p^R(t)&=-i\theta(t)\left\langle\Psi_0\right|\left\{a_p(t),a_p^{\dagger}\right\}\left|\Psi_0\right\rangle\\
&=-i\theta(t)\left[\left\langle\Psi_0\right|a_p(t)\,a_p^{\dagger}\left|\Psi_0\right\rangle+\left\langle\Psi_0\right|a_p^{\dagger}\,a_p(t)\left|\Psi_0\right\rangle\right]\\
&=-i\theta(t)\left[\left\langle\Psi_0\right|e^{iHt}a_pe^{-iHt}a_p^{\dagger}\left|\Psi_0\right\rangle+\left\langle\Psi_0\right|a_p^{\dagger}e^{iHt}a_pe^{-iHt}\left|\Psi_0\right\rangle\right]\\
&=-i\theta(t)\left[e^{iE_0t}\left\langle\Psi_0\right|a_p\,e^{-iHt}\,a_p^{\dagger}\left|\Psi_0\right\rangle+e^{-iE_0t}\left\langle\Psi_0\right|a_p^{\dagger}\,e^{iHt}\,a_p\left|\Psi_0\right\rangle\right]
% &=-i\theta(t)\Big[e^{iE_0t}\left\langle\Psi_0\right|a_p\,e^{-iHt}\,\mathbb{I}_{N+1}\,a_p^{\dagger}\left|\Psi_0\right\rangle+e^{-iE_0t}\left\langle\Psi_0\right|a_p^{\dagger}\,e^{iHt}\,\mathbb{I}_{N-1}\,a_p\left|\Psi_0\right\rangle\Big]\\
% &=-i\theta(t)\Big[e^{iE_0t}\sum_s\left\langle\Psi_0\right|a_p\,e^{-iHt}\left|\Psi_s^{N+1}\right\rangle\left\langle\Psi_s^{N+1}\right|a_p^{\dagger}\left|\Psi_0\right\rangle\nonumber\\
% &\qquad\qquad+e^{-iE_0t}\sum_s\left\langle\Psi_0\right|a_p^{\dagger}\,e^{iHt}\left|\Psi_s^{N-1}\right\rangle\left\langle\Psi_s^{N-1}\right|a_p\left|\Psi_0\right\rangle\Big]\\
% &=-i\theta(t)\Big[\sum_s\left\langle\Psi_0\middle|a_p\middle|\Psi_s^{N+1}\right\rangle\left\langle\Psi_s^{N+1}\middle|a_p^{\dagger}\middle|\Psi_0\right\rangle e^{-i\left(E_s^{N+1}-E_0\right)t}\nonumber\\
% &\qquad\qquad+\sum_s\left\langle\Psi_0\middle|a_p^{\dagger}\middle|\Psi_s^{N-1}\right\rangle\left\langle\Psi_s^{N-1}\middle|a_p\middle|\Psi_0\right\rangle e^{-i\left(E_0-E_s^{N-1}\right)t}\Big]\\
% &=-i\theta(t)\left[\underbrace{\sum_s\left|\left\langle\Psi_s^{N+1}\middle|a_p^{\dagger}\middle|\Psi_0\right\rangle\right|^2e^{-i\left(E_s^{N+1}-E_0\right)t}}_{A_p(t)}+\underbrace{\sum_s\left|\left\langle\Psi_s^{N-1}\middle|a_p\middle|\Psi_0\right\rangle\right|^2e^{-i\left(E_0-E_s^{N-1}\right)t}}_{R_p(t)}\right], 
\label{eq:green_exact}
\end{align}
Partition into the non-interacting Hamiltonian $\hat H_0=\hat H_{\rm el}+\hat H_{\rm pl}$ and the perturbation
 $\hat V=\hat H_{\rm ep}$. We can define our reference state to be $|\Phi\rangle\equiv|{\rm FS}\rangle\otimes|0\rangle_{\rm b}$, where $|\rm FS\rangle$ is the Fermi surface state (the choices for this are HF or LDA) and $|0\rangle_{\rm b}$ is the bosonic vacuum. Then, $\hat H_0|\Phi\rangle=E_\Phi|\Phi\rangle$, with $E_\Phi=\sum_{\bm k\in\mathcal O}\epsilon_{\bm k}^{\rm HF}$.
\subsubsection{Definitions}
Define $\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)\equiv\epsilon_{\bm k+\bm q}-\epsilon_{\bm k}+\Omega_{\nu,-\bm q}>0$ and $\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)\equiv\epsilon_{p+\bm q}-\epsilon_p-\Omega_{\nu,\bm q}$, which use $\bm{k}\in\mathcal O$ with $\bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V$ and $p+\bm q\in\mathcal O$, respectively. We have the Hermiticity condition $g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)^*=g_\nu(\bm k+\bm q,-\bm q)$. The positivity of $\Delta^{\mathcal V}$ in the finite basis ensures that $|\Phi\rangle$ is a non-degenerate ground state of $\hat H_0$, allowing for a well-defined RSPT. The boson parity operator $\hat P=(-1)^{\hat N_b}$ trivially commutes with the fermionic operators $\hat H_{\rm el}$, $a_p$, and $a_p^\dagger$, commutes with $\hat H_{\rm pl}$ (even number of bosons), and anticommutes with $\hat V$ (odd number of bosons). Any matrix element with an odd number of $\hat V$ operators between states of the same parity vanishes.
%  By the fourth line of Eq.~(\ref{eq:green_exact}), the two terms can be written without reference to the $N\pm1$ eigenstates:
% \begin{equation}
% A_p(t)=e^{iE_0t}\left\langle\Psi_0\right|a_p\,e^{-i\hat Ht}\,a_p^\dagger\left|\Psi_0\right\rangle,\qquad
% R_p(t)=e^{-iE_0t}\left\langle\Psi_0\right|a_p^\dagger\,e^{i\hat Ht}\,a_p\left|\Psi_0\right\rangle .
% \end{equation}
\subsubsection{Ground state properties}
With intermediate normalization, $\langle\Phi|\Psi_0\rangle=1$, and defining the reduced resolvent
$\hat R_0\equiv\hat Q\,(E_\Phi-\hat H_0)^{-1}\hat Q$ with $\hat Q\equiv1-|\Phi\rangle\langle\Phi|$, RSPT gives the ground state as $|\Psi_0\rangle=\mathcal N^{-1/2}|\tilde\Psi_0\rangle$, with
\begin{align}
|\tilde\Psi_0\rangle&=|\Phi\rangle+|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle+|\Psi^{(2)}\rangle+O(g^3),\\
|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle&\equiv\hat R_0\hat V|\Phi\rangle\\&
=\sum_{n\neq\Phi}|n\rangle\frac{\langle n|\hat V|\Phi\rangle}{E_\Phi-E_n}\\
&=-\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}\,a^\dagger_{\bm k+\bm q}a_{\bm k}b^\dagger_{\nu_{-\bm q}}|\Phi\rangle,\\
|\Psi^{(2)}\rangle&\equiv\hat R_0\hat V|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle-E_0^{(1)}\hat R_0|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle
=\hat R_0\hat V\hat R_0\hat V|\Phi\rangle\qquad\left(E_0^{(1)}=\langle\Phi|\hat V|\Phi\rangle=0\right)\\
&=\sum_{n\neq\Phi}\sum_{m\neq\Phi}|n\rangle\frac{\langle n|\hat V|m\rangle\langle m|\hat V|\Phi\rangle}{(E_\Phi-E_n)(E_\Phi-E_m)}\\
\mathcal N&\equiv\langle\tilde\Psi_0|\tilde\Psi_0\rangle
\\&=\Big(\langle\Phi|+\langle\Psi^{(1)}|+\langle\Psi^{(2)}|+\dots\Big)\Big(|\Phi\rangle+|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle+|\Psi^{(2)}\rangle+\dots\Big)\\
&=\langle\Phi|\Phi\rangle
+\underbrace{2\,{\rm Re}\,\langle\Phi|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle+2\,{\rm Re}\,\langle\Phi|\Psi^{(2)}\rangle}_{=0\ \text{(intermediate normalization)}}
+\langle\Psi^{(1)}|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle
+\underbrace{2\,{\rm Re}\,\langle\Psi^{(1)}|\Psi^{(2)}\rangle}_{=0\ \text{(boson parity)}}
+O(g^4)\\
&=1+\langle\Psi^{(1)}|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle+O(g^4)\\
&=1+\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)^2}
+\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\frac{|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(p,\bm q)^2}+O(g^4),\\
E_0&\equiv\langle\Phi|\hat H|\tilde\Psi_0\rangle
\qquad\left(\text{since }\hat H|\tilde\Psi_0\rangle=E_0|\tilde\Psi_0\rangle\text{ and }\langle\Phi|\tilde\Psi_0\rangle=1\right)\\
&=\underbrace{\langle\Phi|\hat H_0|\tilde\Psi_0\rangle}_{E_\Phi}+\langle\Phi|\hat V|\tilde\Psi_0\rangle
\\
&=E_\Phi+\underbrace{\langle\Phi|\hat V|\Phi\rangle}_{=0\ \text{(parity)}}+\langle\Phi|\hat V|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle
+\underbrace{\langle\Phi|\hat V|\Psi^{(2)}\rangle}_{=0\ \text{(parity)}}+O(g^4)\\
&=E_\Phi-\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}
\,\langle\Phi|\hat V\,a^\dagger_{\bm k+\bm q}a_{\bm k}b^\dagger_{\nu_{-\bm q}}|\Phi\rangle+O(g^4)\\
&=E_\Phi-\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}
-\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\frac{|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(p,\bm q)}+O(g^4).
\end{align}
\subsection{Electron removal}
We want the removal term to second order in the coupling,
\begin{align}
R_p(t)&=e^{-iE_0t}\left\langle\Psi_0\right|a_p^\dagger\,e^{i\hat Ht}\,a_p\left|\Psi_0\right\rangle\\
&=\frac{e^{-iE_0t}}{\mathcal N}\left\langle\tilde\Psi_0\right|a_p^\dagger\,e^{i\hat Ht}\,a_p\left|\tilde\Psi_0\right\rangle.
\end{align}
To remove the need for constructing any $(N-1)$-electron eigenstates, we must expand the propagator $e^{i\hat Ht}$ acting on the $(N-1)$-electron state to $O(g^2)$. 
\begin{align}
a_p|\tilde\Psi_0\rangle
&=a_p|\Phi\rangle+a_p|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle+a_p|\Psi^{(2)}\rangle+O(g^3)\\
&=\underbrace{|p\rangle}_{O(1)}
\;\underbrace{-\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}\,|p;\bm k\bm q\nu\rangle}_{a_p|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle=O(g)}
\;+\underbrace{a_p|\Psi^{(2)}\rangle}_{O(g^2)}+O(g^3).
\end{align}
Expanding on the identity $e^{i\hat Ht}=e^{i\hat H_0t}+i\int_0^tds\,e^{i\hat H_0(t-s)}\hat V e^{i\hat Hs}$ gives
\begin{align}
e^{i\hat Ht}&=\underbrace{e^{i\hat H_0t}}_{\hat X_0}+\underbrace{i\int_0^t\!ds\,e^{i\hat H_0(t-s)}\hat V e^{i\hat H_0s}}_{\hat X_1}
\underbrace{-\int_0^t\!ds\int_0^s\!ds'\,e^{i\hat H_0(t-s)}\hat V e^{i\hat H_0(s-s')}\hat V e^{i\hat H_0s'}}_{\hat X_2}+O(g^3)\\
\implies \mathcal N\,e^{iE_0t}R_p(t) &=\langle\tilde\Psi_0|a_p^\dagger e^{i\hat Ht}a_p|\tilde\Psi_0\rangle\\
&=\underbrace{{\langle p|\hat X_0|p\rangle}}_{O(1)}\\
&+\underbrace{{\langle p|\hat X_1|p\rangle} +{\langle p|\hat X_0a_p|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle+\langle\Psi^{(1)}|a_p^\dagger\hat X_0|p\rangle}}_{=0 (\text{odd number of }\hat V)}\\
&+\underbrace{{\langle p|\hat X_2|p\rangle+\langle\Psi^{(1)}|a_p^\dagger\hat X_0a_p|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle+\langle p|\hat X_1a_p|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle+\langle\Psi^{(1)}|a_p^\dagger\hat X_1|p\rangle}}_{O(g^2)}\\
&\quad+\underbrace{\langle p|\hat X_0a_p|\Psi^{(2)}\rangle+\langle\Psi^{(2)}|a_p^\dagger\hat X_0|p\rangle}_{O(g^2)}+O(g^4)\\
&=\underbrace{\langle p|\hat X_0+\hat X_2|p\rangle}_{T_0}
+\underbrace{\langle\Psi^{(1)}|a_p^\dagger\hat X_0a_p|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle}_{T_1} + \underbrace{\langle p|\hat X_1a_p|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle+\langle\Psi^{(1)}|a_p^\dagger\hat X_1|p\rangle}_{T_2+T_3}
+O(g^4).
\end{align}
\subsubsection{$T_0$}
First, using $\hat H_0|p\rangle=(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)|p\rangle$:
\begin{align}
    \langle p|\hat X_0|p\rangle&=e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)t}.
\end{align}
Then,
\begin{align}
\langle p|\hat X_2|p\rangle
&=-\int_0^t\!ds\int_0^s\!ds'\,\langle p|e^{i\hat H_0(t-s)}\hat V e^{i\hat H_0(s-s')}\hat V e^{i\hat H_0s'}|p\rangle\\
&=-\int_0^t\!ds\int_0^s\!ds'\,e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)(t-s)}e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)s'}\,\langle p|\hat V e^{i\hat H_0(s-s')}\hat V|p\rangle\\
&=-\int_0^t\!ds\int_0^s\!ds'\,e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)(t-s+s')}\sum_n\langle p|\hat V|n\rangle\,e^{iE_n(s-s')}\,\langle n|\hat V|p\rangle \label{eq:Xp2_3}\\
&=-\,e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)t}\sum_n\left|\langle n|\hat V|p\rangle\right|^2\int_0^t\!ds\int_0^s\!ds'\,e^{i\left[E_n-(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)\right](s-s')} \\
&=e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)t}\Bigg[
\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2\,
\frac{e^{i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)t}-1-i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)t}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)^2}\nonumber\\
&+\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal O}}|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2\,
\frac{e^{-i\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)t}-1+i\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)t}{\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)^2}\Bigg], \label{eq:Xp2_5}
\end{align}
where to get to \ref{eq:Xp2_3} we inserted a complete set of $\hat H_0$ eigenstates $|n\rangle$. To get to \ref{eq:Xp2_5}, define $\omega_n\equiv E_n-(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)$ and evaluate the double integral as
\begin{align}
\int_0^t\!ds\int_0^s\!ds'\,e^{i\omega_n(s-s')}
&=\int_0^t\!ds\,\frac{e^{i\omega_ns}-1}{i\omega_n}
=\frac{1}{i\omega_n}\left[\frac{e^{i\omega_nt}-1}{i\omega_n}-t\right]
=-\,\frac{e^{i\omega_nt}-1-i\omega_nt}{\omega_n^2}.
\end{align}
To understand why only two specific terms survive, write out $\hat V|p\rangle$,
\begin{align}
\hat V|p\rangle&=\sum_{\bm k,\bm q}\sum_\nu g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)\,a^\dagger_{\bm k+\bm q}a_{\bm k}\left(b^\dagger_{\nu_{-\bm q}}+b_{\nu_{\bm q}}\right)a_p|\Phi\rangle\\
&=\sum_{\bm k,\bm q}\sum_\nu g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)\,a^\dagger_{\bm k+\bm q}a_{\bm k}b^\dagger_{\nu_{-\bm q}}a_p|\Phi\rangle\\
&=\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)\,|p;\bm k\bm q\nu\rangle
-\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal O}}g_\nu(p,\bm q)^*\,|m_{\bm q\nu}\rangle ,
\end{align}
where $|p;\bm k\bm q\nu\rangle\equiv a^\dagger_{\bm k+\bm q}a_{\bm k}b^\dagger_{\nu_{-\bm q}}|p\rangle$ and $|m_{\bm q\nu}\rangle\equiv a_{p+\bm q}b^\dagger_{\nu_{\bm q}}|\Phi\rangle$.
Physically, the first term corresponds to the situation where the hole remains in orbital $p$, while another electron is excited. The second term corresponds to the situation where the hole hops from orbital $p$ to orbital $p+\bm q$.
\subsubsection{$T_1$}
\begin{align}
T_1&=\langle\Psi^{(1)}|a_p^\dagger\,\hat X_0\,a_p|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle\\
&=\left(a_p|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle\right)^\dagger e^{i\hat H_0t}\left(a_p|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle\right)\\ \label{eq:T1_3}
&=\sum_{\substack{\bm k'\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k'+\bm q'\in\mathcal V}}\sum_{\nu'}\;
\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu
\frac{g_{\nu'}(\bm k',\bm q')^*\,g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_{\nu'}(\bm k',\bm q')\,\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}\,
\langle p;\bm k'\bm q'\nu'|e^{i\hat H_0t}|p;\bm k\bm q\nu\rangle\\
&=\sum_{\substack{\bm k'\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k'+\bm q'\in\mathcal V}}\sum_{\nu'}\;
\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu
\frac{g_{\nu'}(\bm k',\bm q')^*\,g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_{\nu'}(\bm k',\bm q')\,\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}\,
e^{i\left(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p+\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)\right)t}\,
\delta_{\bm k\bm k'}\delta_{\bm q\bm q'}\delta_{\nu\nu'}\\
&=e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)t}\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu
\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)^2}\,e^{i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)t},
\end{align}
where to get to \ref{eq:T1_3} we used
\begin{align}
a_p|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle
&=-\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}\,a_p\,a^\dagger_{\bm k+\bm q}a_{\bm k}b^\dagger_{\nu_{-\bm q}}|\Phi\rangle \\
&=-\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}\,a^\dagger_{\bm k+\bm q}a_{\bm k}b^\dagger_{\nu_{-\bm q}}\,a_p|\Phi\rangle\\
&\equiv -\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}\,|p;\bm k\bm q\nu\rangle .
\label{eq:apPsi1}
\end{align}
\subsubsection{$T_2$ and $T_3$}
\begin{align}
T_2&=\langle p|\hat X_1\,a_p|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle\\%,\qquad T_3=\langle\Psi^{(1)}|a_p^\dagger\,\hat X_1|p\rangle .
&=i\int_0^t\!ds\,\langle p|e^{i\hat H_0(t-s)}\hat V e^{i\hat H_0s}a_p|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle\\
&=i\int_0^t\!ds\,e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)(t-s)}\sum_n\langle p|\hat V|n\rangle\,e^{iE_ns}\,\langle n|a_p|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle \\ \label{eq:T2_3}
&=i\int_0^t\!ds\,e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)(t-s)}
\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu
g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)^*\left(-\frac{g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}\right)
e^{i\left(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p+\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)\right)s}\\
&=-i\,e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)t}\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu
\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}\int_0^t\!ds\,e^{i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)s}\\
&=-i\,e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)t}\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu
\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}\,
\frac{e^{i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)t}-1}{i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}\\
&=-\,e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)t}\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu
\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)^2}\left(e^{i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)t}-1\right), 
\end{align}
where to get to \ref{eq:T2_3} we used the fact, derived in \ref{eq:apPsi1}, that $\langle n|a_p|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle$ is nonzero only for $|n\rangle=|p;\bm k\bm q\nu\rangle$. Then, 
\begin{align}
T_3&=\langle\Psi^{(1)}|a_p^\dagger\,\hat X_1|p\rangle\\
&=i\int_0^t\!ds\,\langle\Psi^{(1)}|a_p^\dagger\,e^{i\hat H_0(t-s)}\hat V e^{i\hat H_0s}|p\rangle\\
&=i\int_0^t\!ds\,e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)s}\,\langle\Psi^{(1)}|a_p^\dagger\,e^{i\hat H_0(t-s)}\hat V|p\rangle\\
&=i\int_0^t\!ds\,e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)s}\sum_n\langle\Psi^{(1)}|a_p^\dagger|n\rangle\,e^{iE_n(t-s)}\,\langle n|\hat V|p\rangle \\&=i\int_0^t\!ds\,e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)s}
\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu
\left(-\frac{g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)^*}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}\right)
e^{i\left(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p+\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)\right)(t-s)}\,g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)\\
&=-i\,e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)t}\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu
\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}\,e^{i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)t}\int_0^t\!ds\,e^{-i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)s}\\
&=-i\,e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)t}\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu
\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}\,e^{i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)t}\,
\frac{1-e^{-i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)t}}{i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}\\
&=-\,e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)t}\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu
\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)^2}\left(e^{i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)t}-1\right).
\end{align}
Therefore,
\begin{equation}
T_2+T_3=-2\,e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)t}\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu
\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)^2}\left(e^{i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)t}-1\right).
\end{equation}
Putting it all together gives 
\begin{align}
&\mathcal N\,e^{iE_0t}R_p(t)=T_0+T_1+T_2+T_3+O(g^4)\\
&=e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)t}\Bigg[1
+\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)^2}
\Big(\underbrace{e^{i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)t}-1-i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)t}_{T_0}
+\underbrace{e^{i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)t}}_{T_1}
\underbrace{-2e^{i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)t}+2}_{T_2+T_3}\Big)\nonumber\\
&\qquad\qquad+\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal O}}\frac{|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)^2}\left(e^{-i\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)t}+i\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)t-1\right)\Bigg]+O(g^4)\\
&=e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)t}\Bigg[1
+\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)^2}
\left(1-i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)t\right)\nonumber\\
&\qquad\qquad+\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal O}}\frac{|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)^2}\left(e^{-i\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)t}+i\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)t-1\right)\Bigg]+O(g^4).
\end{align}
Now using the identities
\begin{align}
    \mathcal N^{-1}&=1-\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)^2}
-\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\frac{|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(p,\bm q)^2}+O(g^4),\\
e^{-iE_0t}&=e^{-iE_\Phi t}\,e^{-i(E_0-E_\Phi)t}
=e^{-iE_\Phi t}\Bigg[1+it\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}
+it\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\frac{|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(p,\bm q)}\Bigg]+O(g^4),
\end{align}
we find
\begin{align}
R_p(t)&=\mathcal N^{-1}\,e^{-iE_0t}\left[\mathcal N\,e^{iE_0t}R_p(t)\right]\\
&=\underbrace{e^{-iE_\Phi t}\,e^{i(E_\Phi-\epsilon_p)t}}_{=\,e^{-i\epsilon_pt}}
\Bigg[1-\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)^2}
-\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\frac{|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(p,\bm q)^2}\Bigg]_{\mathcal N^{-1}}\nonumber\\
&\qquad\times\Bigg[1+it\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}
+it\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\frac{|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(p,\bm q)}\Bigg]_{e^{-i(E_0-E_\Phi)t}}\nonumber\\
&\qquad\times\Bigg[1+\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)^2}
-it\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}\nonumber\\
&\qquad\qquad+\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal O}}\frac{|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)^2}\left(e^{-i\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)t}+i\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)t-1\right)\Bigg]_{T_0+T_1+T_2+T_3}+O(g^4)\label{eq:R_p_t_final}\\
&=e^{-i\epsilon_pt}\Bigg[1
+\cancel{\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)^2}
-\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)^2}}\nonumber\\
&\qquad\qquad+\cancel{\left(-it\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}
+it\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}\right)}\nonumber\\
&\qquad\qquad{-\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\frac{|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(p,\bm q)^2}}
\;{+\,it\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\frac{|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(p,\bm q)}}\nonumber\\
&\qquad\qquad+{\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal O}}\frac{|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)^2}\left(e^{-i\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)t}+i\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)t-1\right)}\Bigg]+O(g^4)\label{eq:R_p_t_final}\\
&=e^{-i\epsilon_pt}\Bigg[1
+\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal O}}\frac{|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)^2}\left(e^{-i\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)t}+i\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)t-1\right)\nonumber\\
&\qquad\qquad+\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\frac{|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(p,\bm q)^2}\left(i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(p,\bm q)t-1\right)\Bigg]+O(g^4),
\end{align}
where to get to \ref{eq:R_p_t_final}, we used the fact that each bracket is $1+O(g^2)$, so the product of any two $O(g^2)$ pieces is $O(g^4)$. 
\subsection{Electron addition}
Since $p\in\mathcal O$, $a_p^\dagger|\Phi\rangle=0$, and only the $\bm k=p$ terms of $|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle$ survive. Using $a_p^\dagger a^\dagger_{p+\bm q}a_p|\Phi\rangle=-a^\dagger_{p+\bm q}|\Phi\rangle$,
\begin{equation}
a_p^\dagger|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle=\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\frac{g_\nu(p,\bm q)}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(p,\bm q)}\,a^\dagger_{p+\bm q}b^\dagger_{\nu_{-\bm q}}|\Phi\rangle,
\qquad
\hat H_0\,a^\dagger_{p+\bm q}b^\dagger_{\nu_{-\bm q}}|\Phi\rangle=\left(E_\Phi+\epsilon_p+\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(p,\bm q)\right)a^\dagger_{p+\bm q}b^\dagger_{\nu_{-\bm q}}|\Phi\rangle .
\end{equation}
Because $a_p^\dagger|\tilde\Psi_0\rangle=O(g)$, parity implies that every correction to $\langle\Psi^{(1)}|a_p\,e^{-i\hat H_0t}\,a_p^\dagger|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle$ (from $|\Psi^{(2)}\rangle$, from higher terms of $e^{-i\hat Ht}$, from $\mathcal N$, and from $E_0-E_\Phi$) enters at $O(g^4)$. Hence
\begin{equation}
A_p(t)=e^{iE_\Phi t}\langle\Psi^{(1)}|a_p\,e^{-i\hat H_0t}\,a_p^\dagger|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle+O(g^4)
=e^{-i\epsilon_pt}\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\frac{|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(p,\bm q)^2}\,e^{-i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(p,\bm q)t}+O(g^4).
\label{eq:addition_final}
\end{equation}

\subsubsection{Landau form}
Add Eqs.~(\ref{eq:removal_final}) and (\ref{eq:addition_final}) and divide by $G_p^{R,0}(t)=-i\theta(t)e^{-i\epsilon_pt}$. The addition sector supplies the missing $e^{-i\Delta t}$ for the virtual branch, so both branches take the same form:
\begin{align}
\frac{G_p^R(t)}{G_p^{R,0}(t)}
=1&+\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal O}}\frac{|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)^2}\left(e^{-i\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)t}+i\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)t-1\right)\nonumber\\
&+\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\frac{|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(p,\bm q)^2}\left(e^{-i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(p,\bm q)t}+i\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(p,\bm q)t-1\right)+O(g^4).
\end{align}
Introducing the excitation spectral function
\begin{equation}
\beta_p(\omega)\equiv\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal O}}|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2\,\delta\!\left(\omega-\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)\right)
+\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2\,\delta\!\left(\omega-\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(p,\bm q)\right),
\end{equation}
this becomes
\begin{equation}
\frac{G_p^R(t)}{G_p^{R,0}(t)}=1+\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\omega\,\frac{\beta_p(\omega)}{\omega^2}\left(e^{-i\omega t}+i\omega t-1\right)+O(g^4).
\end{equation}
Defining the cumulant through $G_p^R(t)\equiv G_p^{R,0}(t)\,e^{C_p(t)}$, and using $\ln(1+x)=x+O(x^2)$ with $x=O(g^2)$,
\begin{equation}
C_p(t)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\omega\,\frac{\beta_p(\omega)}{\omega^2}\left(e^{-i\omega t}+i\omega t-1\right)+O(g^4),
\end{equation}
which is the Landau form. Truncating at second order gives the retarded G0W0+C Green's function,
\begin{equation}
G_p^R(t)\approx-i\theta(t)\,e^{-i\epsilon_pt}\exp\!\left[\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\omega\,\frac{\beta_p(\omega)}{\omega^2}\left(e^{-i\omega t}+i\omega t-1\right)\right].
\end{equation}
 Define 
$D_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)\equiv\epsilon_{\bm k+\bm q}-\epsilon_{\bm k}+\Omega_{\nu,-\bm q}>0$ for $\bm k\in\mathcal O$, $\bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V$.
Define
\begin{gather}
|p\rangle\equiv a_p|\Phi\rangle,\qquad E_p^{(0)}\equiv E_\Phi-\epsilon_p,\qquad
K(\omega,t)\equiv\frac{e^{-i\omega t}+i\omega t-1}{\omega^2},\\
\Delta^{\mathcal V}_\nu(p,\bm q)\equiv\epsilon_{p+\bm q}-\epsilon_p+\Omega_{\nu,-\bm q}\ \ (p+\bm q\in\mathcal V),\qquad
\Delta^{\mathcal O}_\nu(p,\bm q)\equiv\epsilon_{p+\bm q}-\epsilon_p-\Omega_{\nu\bm q}\ \ (p+\bm q\in\mathcal O).
\end{gather}
We abbreviate three sums. $\sum_{\rm sp}$ runs over $\bm k\in\mathcal O\setminus\{p\}$, $\bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V$, and $\nu$, with $g\equiv g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)$ and $D\equiv D_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)$. $\sum_{\mathcal X}$ runs over $(\bm q,\nu)$ with $p+\bm q\in\mathcal X$, with $g\equiv g_\nu(p,\bm q)$ and $\Delta\equiv\Delta^{\mathcal X}_\nu(p,\bm q)$.
Since $\hat P=(-1)^{\hat N_b}$ commutes with $\hat H_0$ and $a_p$ but anticommutes with $\hat V$, every odd-order term below vanishes, and all remainders are $O(g^4)$ at fixed $t$.
\paragraph{Ground state.}
In RSPT with intermediate normalization, $|\tilde\Psi_0\rangle=|\Phi\rangle+|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle+|\Psi^{(2)}\rangle+O(g^3)$, $\langle\Phi|\Psi^{(n)}\rangle=0$, and
\begin{align}
|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle&=-\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}{D_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}\,a^\dagger_{\bm k+\bm q}a_{\bm k}b^\dagger_{\nu_{-\bm q}}|\Phi\rangle,\\
\mathcal N&\equiv\langle\tilde\Psi_0|\tilde\Psi_0\rangle=1+\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu \frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{|D_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}+\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{|\Delta_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}+O(g^4)\\
E_0&=E_\Phi-\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{D_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}-\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{|g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)|^2}{\Delta_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}+O(g^4).
\end{align}
Then, the normalized ground state is $|\Psi_0\rangle=\mathcal N^{-1/2}|\tilde\Psi_0\rangle$.
\paragraph{Removal.}
Acting with $a_p$ kills the $\bm k=p$ terms of $|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle$.
%  With $|p;\bm k\bm q\nu\rangle\equiv a^\dagger_{\bm k+\bm q}a_{\bm k}b^\dagger_{\nu_{-\bm q}}|p\rangle$ and $|m_{\bm q\nu}\rangle\equiv a_{p+\bm q}b^\dagger_{\nu_{\bm q}}|\Phi\rangle$, both orthonormal $\hat H_0$ eigenstates,
\begin{align}
% a_p|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle&=-\sum_{\rm sp}\frac{g}{D}\,|p;\bm k\bm q\nu\rangle,\qquad
a_p|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle&=-\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu\frac{g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}{D_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)}\,a^\dagger_{\bm k+\bm q}a_{\bm k}b^\dagger_{\nu_{-\bm q}}a_p|\Phi\rangle\\
\hat V a_p|\Phi\rangle&=-\sum_{\substack{\bm q,\nu\\ p+\bm q\in\mathcal O}}g_\nu(p,\bm q)^*\,|m_{\bm q\nu}\rangle +\sum_{\substack{\bm k\in\mathcal O\\ \bm k+\bm q\in\mathcal V}}\sum_\nu g_\nu(\bm k,\bm q)\,a^\dagger_{\bm k+\bm q}a_{\bm k}b^\dagger_{\nu_{-\bm q}}a_p|\Phi\rangle,
% \hat V|p\rangle=\sum_{\rm sp}g\,|p;\bm k\bm q\nu\rangle-\sum_{\mathcal O}g^*\,|m_{\bm q\nu}\rangle,
\end{align}
with excitation energies $D$ and $-\Delta^{\mathcal O}$ relative to $E_p^{(0)}$. Iterate the Duhamel identity $e^{i\hat Ht}=e^{i\hat H_0t}+i\int_0^tds\,e^{i\hat H_0(t-s)}\hat V e^{i\hat Hs}$ to obtain $\hat X_0+\hat X_1+\hat X_2+O(g^3)$. By parity, and using $\langle\Psi^{(2)}|a_p^\dagger|p\rangle=\langle\Psi^{(2)}|\Phi\rangle=0$,
\begin{align}
\mathcal N\,e^{iE_0t}R_p(t)
&=\langle p|\hat X_0+\hat X_2|p\rangle+\langle\Psi^{(1)}|a_p^\dagger\hat X_0a_p|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle
+\langle\Psi^{(1)}|a_p^\dagger\hat X_1|p\rangle+\langle p|\hat X_1a_p|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle+O(g^4)\\
&=e^{iE_p^{(0)}t}\Bigg[1+\sum_{\rm sp}|g|^2K(-D,t)+\sum_{\mathcal O}|g|^2K(\Delta,t)
+\sum_{\rm sp}\frac{|g|^2}{D^2}e^{iDt}-2\sum_{\rm sp}\frac{|g|^2}{D^2}\left(e^{iDt}-1\right)\Bigg]+O(g^4)\\
&=e^{iE_p^{(0)}t}\Bigg[1+S-it\sum_{\rm sp}\frac{|g|^2}{D}+\sum_{\mathcal O}|g|^2K(\Delta,t)\Bigg]+O(g^4).
\end{align}
In the last step the spectator terms collapse to $|g|^2(1-iDt)/D^2$. Multiplying by $\mathcal N^{-1}e^{-iE_0t}$ cancels $S$ and the spectator secular term, leaving
\begin{equation}
R_p(t)=e^{-i\epsilon_pt}\Bigg[1+\sum_{\mathcal O}|g|^2K(\Delta,t)+\sum_{\mathcal V}\frac{|g|^2}{\Delta^2}\left(i\Delta t-1\right)\Bigg]+O(g^4).
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Addition.}
Since $a_p^\dagger|\Phi\rangle=0$, only the $\bm k=p$ terms of $|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle$ survive, and $a_p^\dagger a^\dagger_{p+\bm q}a_p|\Phi\rangle=-a^\dagger_{p+\bm q}|\Phi\rangle$. Because $a_p^\dagger|\tilde\Psi_0\rangle=O(g)$, parity leaves only $\hat X_0$ at this order:
\begin{equation}
a_p^\dagger|\Psi^{(1)}\rangle=\sum_{\mathcal V}\frac{g}{\Delta}\,a^\dagger_{p+\bm q}b^\dagger_{\nu_{-\bm q}}|\Phi\rangle
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
A_p(t)=e^{-i\epsilon_pt}\sum_{\mathcal V}\frac{|g|^2}{\Delta^2}\,e^{-i\Delta t}+O(g^4).
\end{equation}

\paragraph{Landau form.}
Adding the two sectors, the virtual branch completes the kernel $K$:
\begin{equation}
\frac{G_p^R(t)}{G_p^{R,0}(t)}=1+\sum_{\mathcal X\in\{\mathcal O,\mathcal V\}}\sum_{\mathcal X}|g|^2K(\Delta,t)+O(g^4)
=1+\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\omega\,\beta_p(\omega)\,\frac{e^{-i\omega t}+i\omega t-1}{\omega^2}+O(g^4),
\end{equation}
where $G_p^{R,0}(t)=-i\theta(t)e^{-i\epsilon_pt}$ and
$\beta_p(\omega)\equiv\sum_{\mathcal X}\sum_{\mathcal X}|g_\nu(p,\bm q)|^2\,\delta\big(\omega-\Delta^{\mathcal X}_\nu(p,\bm q)\big)$.
Defining $C_p(t)\equiv\ln[G_p^R(t)/G_p^{R,0}(t)]=C_p^{(2)}(t)+O(g^4)$ and truncating at second order gives
\begin{equation}
G_p^R(t)\approx-i\theta(t)\,e^{-i\epsilon_pt}\exp\!\left[\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}d\omega\,\frac{\beta_p(\omega)}{\omega^2}\left(e^{-i\omega t}+i\omega t-1\right)\right].
\end{equation}
 The effective Hamiltonian can be diagonalized by introducing shifted bosonic operators
\begin{equation}
\widetilde B_{pm\nu}
=
B_{pm\nu}
-
\beta_{pm\nu},
\qquad
\widetilde B_{pm\nu}^{\dagger}
=
B_{pm\nu}^{\dagger}
-
\beta_{pm\nu}^{*},
\end{equation}
where, analogous to section \ref{sec:polaron_ground_state_energy}, the coherent displacements are determined as
\begin{equation}
\beta_{pm\nu}
=
-
\frac{\mathcal{M}_{pm\nu}}
{\Delta_{pm\nu}}.
\end{equation}
This allows us to rewrite the Hamiltonian as
\begin{equation}
H_p^{\rm eff}
=
E_p^{\rm QP}
+
\sum_{m\nu}
\Delta_{pm\nu}
\widetilde B_{pm\nu}^{\dagger}
\widetilde B_{pm\nu},
\end{equation}
with
\begin{align}
E_p^{\rm QP}
&=
\epsilon_p^{\rm HF}
-
\sum_{m\nu}
\frac{|\mathcal{M}_{pm\nu}|^2}
{\Delta_{pm\nu}}
\\
&=
\epsilon_p^{\rm HF}
+ \Sigma_p^{\rm GW}(\epsilon_p^{\rm HF}).
\end{align}
As in section \ref{subsec:RPA_vacuum_coherent_state}, we find that the RPA vacuum is a coherent state of the shifted operators,
\begin{align}
\left(
\widetilde B_{pm\nu}
+
\beta_{pm\nu}
\right)
|0_{\rm RPA}\rangle
&=
0,
\\
\widetilde B_{pm\nu}
|0_{\rm RPA}\rangle
&=
-
\beta_{pm\nu}
|0_{\rm RPA}\rangle.
\end{align}
The retarded Green's function therefore becomes
\begin{align}
G_p^{GW,R}(t)
&=
-i\theta(t)
\langle
0_{\rm RPA}
|
e^{-iH_p^{\rm eff}t}
|
0_{\rm RPA}
\rangle
\\
&=
-i\theta(t)
e^{-iE_p^{\rm QP}t}
\left\langle
0_{\rm RPA}
\left|
e^{-i\sum_{m\nu}
\Delta_{pm\nu}
\widetilde B_{pm\nu}^{\dagger}
\widetilde B_{pm\nu}t}
\right|
0_{\rm RPA}
\right\rangle
\\
&=
-i\theta(t)
e^{-iE_p^{\rm QP}t}
\exp
\left[
\sum_{m\nu}
|\beta_{pm\nu}|^2
\left(
e^{-i\Delta_{pm\nu}t}
-
1
\right)
\right]\\
&=
-i\theta(t)
Z_p
e^{-iE_p^{\rm QP}t}
\prod_{m\nu}
\exp
\left[
|\beta_{pm\nu}|^2
e^{-i\Delta_{pm\nu}t}
\right]
\\
&=
-i\theta(t)
Z_p
\sum_{\mathbf n}
\left[
\prod_{m\nu}
\frac{
|\beta_{pm\nu}|^{2n_{pm\nu}}
}{
n_{pm\nu}!
}
\right]
\exp
\left[
-i
\left(
E_p^{\rm QP}
+
\sum_{m\nu}
n_{pm\nu}
\Delta_{pm\nu}
\right)
t
\right],
\end{align}
where $
Z_p
=
\exp
\left[
-
\sum_{m\nu}
|\beta_{pm\nu}|^2
\right].
$ This happens to be the G0W0+C Green's function. I am wondering, though, why we even care if we solve \ref{eq:Heff}
exactly with G0W0+C. What is the relationship between this independent boson Hamiltonian with the effective excitation energies and the original quasiboson Hamiltonian, where the fermionic portion of the Hamiltonian is a sum over all available states?
